158

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

159

5. How to start with Blockchain Organizing?
Because designing, setting up and managing blockchain organizations is not a daily
job for most people, and requires a lot of expertise, more and more often, blockchain
professionals are deployed. An expert in Blockchain Organizing is competent in devising, designing, setting up a blockchain organization. A good design is important
to prevent problems in the implementation later on. Blockchain Organizing means
that you already consider during the design, that you will cooperate more and more
with your environment and less and less with your ‘regular’ colleagues. It takes into
account that you can mechanize more processes. Plus, Blockchain Organizing does
not only consider the smallest building blocks and the connections between them:
Blockchain Organizing takes this as its fundamental design principle.
When solving management problems within or between organizations you often
hear: ‘we must cooperate better or smarter’. But often this is not possible in a satisfied
way. The experience is that cooperating is difficult and usually leads to many meetings and unfinished action lists. Budgets and lead times are exceeded and cooperation
rarely leads to the desired result. How could this happen? The problem with cooperation is that we take our own company as the center of attention and principle.
People base their thinking on their own company and our own interest. Companies
make products or develop services with the intention to sell them. Organizations
reason from their own supply and then look for customers to go with this. When we
take the company as the unit of analysis and design, we can distinguish the following
partnerships:
1. Within existing institutions157: teams, departments, business units, concern.
2. Between existing institutions: joint venture, alliance, franchising, etcetera.
3. Within (newly set up) partnerships: cooperatives, communities, shared networks,
shared service centers and blockchains.
Organization principles play an important role in all these forms. The main principle
of organizing and managing is that there is always specialization, coordination and
finalization. The still prevailing thought is that the coordinating function should lie
with firms with classic management, traditional markets or governments, but it is
better to reason from the demand and the smallest building blocks and to look for
coordinating capacity that best suits this. This can be a hierarchy or a market, but
also a hybrid in the shape of a shared transaction network for example that uses
blockchain principles. Blockchain Organizing is often a much more efficient way to
coordinate and to reduce insecurity and therefore a better way to cooperate.
Especially the latter form (newly set up partnerships) can be organized well with
Blockchain Organizing. It is a form whereby the smallest building blocks and
157 An institute is a collective name for companies, institutions, independent administrative and

governmental bodies. To make this easier to read, we sometimes replaced the term ‘institute’ by
‘company’ because many government institutions, for example, are also organized like a company.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

160

organizing cooperation between humans and resources is already considered at the
design phase, whereby people not necessarily need to have a permanent ‘connection’
(for example a permanent employment contract) with one or a few fiscal-legal entities
(for example a company). It is possible but not necessary. Permanent connections,
such as: positions, employment contracts, collective employment agreements and
management development programs of companies, have more and more disadvantages. Blockchain Organizing does not have this problem. It does not take the
existing as starting point (there is a company with a building and permanent employees and objectives are sought for this...) but the demand, the need, the problem to
solve. The existing organizations or organizational forms are not important with
Blockchain Organizing, but the coordinating capacity between demand (finalizing:
target and task setting) and supply (specialists: man and resource) is.
In this chapter we discuss some aspects that you must be aware of when you start
with Blockchain Organizing in your organization or network, such as:
▪ What is a blockchain lead and what does he or she do?
▪ The design and development of a business ecosystem
▪ How do you start with Blockchain Organizing?
▪ Project-based approach
▪ Dealing with resistances
5.1 Blockchain lead
We advise larger company’s en network organizations to start with appointing a
blockchain lead. This person will keep track of blockchain developments and assist
directors with questions like: What does Blockchain Organizing mean for our business and what should we do with it and what not? Of course, you can decide to do
nothing, to sit back and see how the future develops. You can catch a ride with other
people, not knowing when you will arrive at your destination. But maybe you also
believe that it is wise to keep your eyes on the road and your hands on the steering
wheel and to determine yourself which decisions to make. To get people interested
in your blockchain innovation you must be able to provide a clear picture of what
the future will bring for your business and how you want to get there.
A blockchain lead158 is the first designated person within an organization or network
to keep track of blockchain developments, to advice the board of directors and management about (dis)investments and to set up and guide projects. An internal/external blockchain lead prepares an organization to use Blockchain Organizing and is
therefore some sort of quartermaster. Some examples of tasks:
▪ keeping track of developments, visiting conferences, building a network;
▪ start a Community of Practice;
▪ designing and setting up Proof of Concept projects and experiments;
158 See for example also Weconet Blockchain Academy on website weconet.org

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

161

▪ creating conditions for a successful implementation;
▪ identifying and developing common values, principles, perspectives, targets, vision and ambitions;
▪ coordinating different internal roles like: purchasing, sales, administration, legal
affairs, ICT and HRM;
▪ alignment with external roles such as: consultants, architects, developers;
▪ project implementation, including: planning, documentation, business model development, prototype delivery.
Many managers want to use blockchain technology within their organization to make
the company stronger than the competition. They want to continue to exist as a
company, while a company is only a tool to produce welfare. Management is an important part of a company and management is mainly a tool to coordinate (human)
recourses to a target. And a large part of traditional management tasks can be transferred from a social to a technical ‘script’, so traditional management becomes less
relevant. And when traditional management becomes less relevant we must stop using it. The competition between Real Madrid and Barcelona becomes irrelevant when
people stop watching soccer.
Traditional companies become less relevant when mechanical management tasks can
be put in a protocol. A blockchain lead first looks at the intention of the tool he
wants to use. Blockchain leadership is not about leadership whereby an IT manager
or CIO knows everything about digitalization or successfully leads an IT company.
Not the technology should be primary, but the question how you can prevent and
solve management problems with technology. Blockchain leadership currently
mainly means that you dare to be a pioneer, think differently, ‘be a cross-thinker’ and
dare to renew. A pioneer is not someone who asks people to follow him or her, but
who challenges them to join the expedition.
Despite our structured approach to realize shared transaction networks, for many
organizations the use of Blockchain Organizing is currently still mainly pioneering.
Blockchain Organizing is so new that you cannot expect that you can use a navigation
system. There are no road signs yet, sometimes not even a road or path. You could
see blockchain technology as a Tesla, but the way how we manage organizations is
still a dirt road. As we already concluded in the introduction of this book, we need
to reinvent management as we know it today. Therefore, we require pioneers who
are not afraid to deviate from the beaten paths and take a new path. A path is created
when you walk on it. The more people subsequently walk on this path, the bigger the
chance that it becomes a road and eventually is included in the route planner.
A blockchain lead is currently mainly a person who learns and shares the accrued
knowledge and experience. Blockchain still requires a lot of failures in order to become successful. But if you are not prepared to fail, you probably also not come up
with original ideas and applications. We must also share failure knowledge. And the
better we share our experiences, the easier it gets to put Blockchain Organizing on

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

162

the map together, and therefore fundamentally innovate management. Blockchain
Organizing can still make the promise of internet come true: a tool to become more
productive so we can sustain our welfare.
In this paragraph we will further discuss the type of leadership that we believe is
required for Blockchain Organizing. We mainly need leadership with the insight that
digitization is a tool to solve management problems. In addition, we mainly need
authentic leadership. That is leadership whereby followers have enough trust that
what their leader says and writes, can also be implemented reliably. People should
know what they can expect and which character, which knowledge and experience
contributes to the identification and solving of management problems. An authentic
leader is also original and pristine and averse of self-evidence, status and linear thinking.
Organic versus mechanic organizing
An important insight into the organizational theory comes from sociologist Tom
Burns and psychologist G. M. Stalker: mechanical versus organic organization. According to Burns and Stalker, the connection between people in mechanical organizations lies mainly in the (decision) structure, hierarchy and formal agreements. This
can increasingly be organized with blockchain as a coordinating mechanism and is
industry agnostic. The connection between people in organic organizations lies
mainly in shared values, perspectives, principles, objectives, governance and culture.
For this we need specific leadership: leaders who know how things work in a certain
industry or organization, in a certain context. It is not one or the other, but depending
on the situations you choose the most appropriate coordinating capacity. According
to the contingency theory, the best organizational form depends on the environment
in which the organization operates. Digital leaders synchronize the way they organize
trust and work with the possibilities that technology offers, in order to solve real
social problems.
Digital leadership requires vision
According to a research by Bart van Ark159, the rapid growth of ICT investments
and the ‘New Digital Economy’ (including mobile, cloud computing and internet),
has not yet given us a visible improvement in the productivity growth. According to
van Ark, this is because ICT is still in the installation stage and will only improve
productivity when ICT is in the deployment or implementation stage. According to
us, we are still hunter and collector when it concerns information. ICT will first have
to be organized industrially (for example with an internet assembly line for data), to
be able to sufficiently contribute to the productivity growth that we need. Blockchain
Organizing will ensure that we move towards a conveyor belt of nodes that cooperate
smartly via a protocol and automatically process transactions and perform certain
management tasks. To make this process from traditional to industrial go smoothly
159 See article by Bart van Ark, The Conference Board and University of Groningen: 'The

Productivity Paradox of the New Digital Economy' (autumn 2016).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

163

we have less need for opportunistic IT suppliers and more need for authentic blockchain leadership. We mainly need leaders who ensure that we use technology smarter
to solve management problems. Therefore, we do not just need new technology, but
first and foremost also another organizational model and paradigm shift. For this
paradigm shift we need binding leadership at all levels.
We think, to quote Otto Scharmer160 that most people know indeed what they want
and what the ‘emerging future’ is. We move from an industrial community to a welfare community, from more to better. Especially care for purpose, people and planet,
whereby profit is a tool to be able to invest. Many people assume that technology
will solve the problems that we have caused ourselves as human beings. For some
people technology has become the new belief system; they sacredly believe in robotics and that technique will save our planet. However, technology itself can do little
about it, it is man who uses technology. For example, when we invented the internal
combustion engine, we had no idea what burning fossil fuels would do to earth and
that it would cause a number of disruptive problems (global warming, climate refugees, etcetera).
It is much wiser not to just use technology to solve problems, but mainly think in
advance which problems can be caused by technology. You can only do this based
on a certain value system. This also means attention to the moral and ethical side of
technology. This means that there are three other organizational perspectives, in addition to technology, economy and politics, that are important: psychology, ecology
and sociology. Blockchain technology could solve but also cause problems. If we
only use Blockchain Organizing to make us more productive, but do not think about
what we do with the surplus time, this can, for example, result in many people sitting
at home without a future, unable to find a meaningful way to spend the available
spare time. Currently it is more important, as we believe John Maynard Keynes meant
it in his article ‘Economics possibilities for our grandchildren’, to think about the question
what we will do with the surplus time, than to think about whether we have sufficient
technology to become more productive.
Trust in leadership at the lowest point
According to the Edelman Trust Barometer, 53% of the worldwide population no
longer trusts existing leaders and institutions. Another 32% is not sure. Only 15%
trusts existing institutions including politics, government, media and companies.
Two thirds of the countries they are investigating are now 'distrusters'. This means
that the trust in the mainstream institutions has dropped below 50%. People no
longer trust that these institutions act in ways that benefit the population. They no
longer belief that the current system still works for him or her. A vast majority of the
people have not enough trust in the institutions we invented to organize trust! And
further globalization and technological changes only reinforces this image. According

160 Otto Scharmer (1961-) American professor and creator of the Theory-U.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

164

to the distrusters, the system fails in leadership and in more protection of the population against for example globalization and technologic renewal.
There is growing doubt about the future and the question whether our children will
have a better life. Many people have lost their faith in systems and organizations. The
only way to regain this trust is through people. People we know, people who are
authentic, competent, people we can trust. Authentic leadership is more important
than ever. Authentic means that statements and deeds must correspond. We need
new leadership and more specifically a new and authentic digital leadership. New
means that we must dare to say goodbye to the existing management models that no
longer work in favor of us, but against us.
Being good and competent in your field of work is therefore not sufficient for a
blockchain lead. We also require leaders who have, for example, a vision on the purpose of work and our identity with economized work. We need leaders who, together
with others, dare to face questions like:
▪ how do we relate to technology as humans?
▪ what does digitization mean and how can we best organize it?
▪ what are the consequences of digitization on organizing trust?
▪ how will the new digital network communities and society look like?
A blockchain leads recognizes the trend that the organization of trust, work and
economy will be digitized more and more and will be organized in both local and
global communities. He or she sees the consequences of this, not just for IT systems,
but mainly for the existing IT architectures, organizational forms, management models, economic models and our social-economic order. As indicated before in this
book, we develop more and more towards a digital society. Digitization starts from
the foundations: how do I organize demand and supply of data at minimum transaction costs? Digitization does not just mean the replacement of analogue processes,
but mainly organizing, for example, privacy, power, democracy and news provision,
differently. This requires a digital strategy and a wide and thorough understanding of
new management models, technical concepts and transition programs.
Together on expedition
Digitization happens in almost all aspects of management and organizations. Almost
all positions are faced with digitization. The question here is not so much about the
differences between digitization for management, sales or HR for example, but
mainly concerns what the similarities are? What are the deeper underlying principles
and how can you digitize from the smallest building blocks and basic processes,
something almost any coordination of supply and demand entails. A blockchain lead
thinks and works from the core and from organizational principles.
With this way of thinking he/she determines a direction, a strategy how to get there
and persuades others to join the expedition. After an organization has gone on an
expedition together with its stakeholders and has created a successful Proof of

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

165

Concept application, the blockchain lead will start to further develop Blockchain Organizing and will eventually implement this. Not only within the own organization,
but mainly through transferring existing processes to a new blockchain organization,
that is part of a shared transaction network. A blockchain lead thinks from a new
frame of mind and knows which management problems must be solved. A blockchain lead has sufficient knowledge and experience and knows the digital concepts
that are required, or knows to find these. New digital leadership knows how to find
specialists and how to manage these in a modern way, with a peer-to-peer platform
and how to use these at minimum transactions costs to achieve better results.
Blockchain leadership
You can vest blockchain leadership with a project leader, community manager or
blockchain lead. A blockchain lead knows how to assess the organization on questions like: What is our function in a network economy? Or: Which processes can I
best do myself, which ones should I outsource and which ones fit better in a shared
network? According to our research, a blockchain lead investigates what the organization must do and which competences must be developed to use Blockchain Organizing. A digital leader new style is aware of the fact that employees, customers
and citizens want to communicate and coordinate differently from how they do it
now and want to be less dependent on existing companies with their own websites,
applications and databases. They no longer want to reason from the company as
center of doing business, but from the individual.
But what does this mean for the connections between organizations and their stakeholders? Which fundamental adjustment is required from the management model,
governance, infrastructure and culture? A blockchain lead wonders how New Organizing can continue to provide effective added value at minimum transaction costs
and what the role of digitization, and more specific Blockchain Organizing, can be
in this. A blockchain lead thinks in hybrid forms: not just doing things yourself or
outsourcing, but also considering a crossbreed of company and market in a shared
network.
More and more blockchain communities will be established, all with their specific
needs in terms of management, coordination and communication and therefore with
a different leadership and management. A blockchain lead must prepare the management for participation in network organizations. And finally, a blockchain lead must
of course be aware of opportunities and threats. Currently, we mainly find threats in
terms of cybersecurity with wiretapping, extortion and data leaks. A blockchain lead
will not only respond to this with practical solutions, but mainly with the installation
of fundamental safeguards to prevent problems. A suitable leader knows for example
that owning data is not important, but access to data is. Or more specific: organizations don’t need ownership of data, even not access to data: they need answers to
question to processes requests. A digital leader is interested in answering questions
to be able to execute own processes. A true digital leader is not interested in owning
data that you are not allowed to use.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

166

Important features of a blockchain lead
A blockchain lead must be able to deal with often complex projects that lead to the
development and setup of a shared transaction network. These are projects with often ten to twenty different parties and stakeholders involved, all with different interests and looking at the problem from different perspectives. Plus, the focus of the
project is not always clear enough in the beginning. But to achieve a focus the leader
cannot suffice by only stating what the focus is. Via a process of steering and letting
go, of control and trust, the group must become focused. This requires authentic
digital leadership.
A leader must take initiative, make participants think from the total, rather than from
their own interest, not give up easily and keep the deployed transition program in
place. The leader must dare to let go of control, to give space to self-organizing capacity of project members. Control is not the same as discipline. Discipline is required from both the leader as well as the participants. This means: honor your agreements (effort obligation). Especially the leader should know, and express this, that
discipline can keep the group together on the often, difficult expeditions.
Authentic leadership requires a leader who can make connections, not just between
the group members, but also externally and in terms of the ten core connections of
a community. A leader is someone who knows that work is being done on a structural
solution of management problems and that it will require a long road to go, with
small steps forwards and sometimes a few steps backwards. Blockchain Organizing
mainly requires a leader who knows that there is often not even a road yet and that
the group will enter territory where nobody has gone before. This can cause confusion, anxiety and sometimes raise fear among the project members. The leader is a
pioneer who is creative and knows ways to achieve the necessary clarity. Someone
who, together with his group, can find the right way to the final destination,.
Within your own organization, you, as blockchain lead, develop an integral vision
and strategy for a digital transformation of your organization, to a shared transaction
network. You are able to make colleagues aware, to develop new frame of minds and
to share new insights with others. You also have the capacity to convince others of
your vision and your organizational plan and how to realize this. Your organizational
plan, your knowledge and experience make your digital strategy concrete. Furthermore, it is especially important to get the right people interested from the start to
realize the desired digital transformation.
In summary, we can formulate some important properties for a(n) (authentic) blockchain lead:
▪ Authenticity: trust your own common sense and do not succumb to the investor
with a large wallet or a director with a budget that has other interests. You are first
and foremost a leader of yourself. According to Mahatma Gandhi, our greatness
as a human being lies not so much in the capacity to re-create the world but in the

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

167

capacity to recreate ourselves. Your working method must be a reflection of your
own personality. Digital transformations and transferring processes to blockchain
organizations will not always be easy. When it matters you show character and
persistence and you trust your values, vision, principles and your personal ambition.
▪ Integrity: digital leadership is also moral leadership and observing codes of conduct and general agreements that have been agreed in your community. You are
transparent and you allow an independent third party as supervisor into your community.
▪ Consistency: you must show consistent behavior. Your behavior must correspond
with your statements and principles. This requires courage. Can others always rely
on you, also in difficult times? Because courage is not the absence of fear but the
judgement that something else is more important than fear.
▪ Authority: be regarded as a recognized expert in a specific area such as Blockchain
Organizing for a certain industry. Ensure to accrue sufficient knowledge and experience that corresponds with your ‘personal brand’. Develop a reputation management system within your community in which you also participate.
▪ Distinctive: distinguish yourself based on your ‘leadership brand’. Your qualities,
knowledge and experience must be expressed in a distinctive way. A way that is
not just different from that of your competitors, but that also provides added
value. When you show your profile clearly and honestly, your target group will
also be able to quickly understand what you stand for.
▪ Relevant and original: what you stand for must be connected to what is important
for you and for others. Do not be a parrot, but come up with original and innovative solutions. Provide added value and relate this value to solving organizational issues, not just a higher yield for shareholders. Do not adjust yourself to the
world, but challenge others to follow you in your vision and organizational plan
▪ Visible: Your leadership must be carried out in a continuous, consistent and repeated way, until it is embedded in the heads of the target group. This involves
repetition and long-term exposure.
▪ Persistence: your leadership needs time to grow. It must be organically developed.
You must persist, not give up, believe in yourself and be patient: “A river cuts through
rock, not because of its power, but because of its persistence.” (James Watkins).
Repairing the roof when the sun is shining
The time has come for radical changes in the way we organize trust, work and economy. Time for change in thinking, time for changing instruments that we use to reduce uncertainty and time for other leadership. A blockchain lead will play an important role in this within Blockchain Organizing. Now is the time to show what you
are worth as blockchain lead for your organization, your network and the community
you are part of. In 2017 and the beginning of 2018 the economy is doing well again
and people will think: why would we change anything? But when the economy is
doing badly, there are other priorities and you do not have time to repair your system.
As the saying goes: “You must repair the roof when the sun is shining”. Now it is definitely

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

168

time to repair your roof, but it is even better to build a new house if your roof is
leaking because the foundation is rotten. Now is the moment to invest in new organizational forms and in blockchain technology.
Blockchain Organizing starts with an idea. Subsequently you want to test how this
idea, or your innovation, is received in the ecosystem you are part of. You could start
this testing phase for example by organizing a kick-off meeting whereby you invite
stakeholders to present them your idea. This kick-off might be the start of a project
to realize a new business ecosystem in which you can usefully apply blockchain technology. To illustrate a concrete approach of a blockchain project, we’ll discuss the
PLATO case: a real project to prepare your organization integrally and ‘by design’
for the new European Privacy law (GDPR) in 2018.
PLATO case
In this chapter we take a concrete Weconet project that we support as consultants.
It is an example to clarify Blockchain Organizing and the way you can (project-related) approach this. The project is called ‘PLATO’ and is named after the insight of
the Greek philosopher Plato: “The cure of a part should not be attempted without treatment
of the whole”. The project is about the smart cooperation to realize a better design of
our data sovereignty and our privacy protection. As indicated before, there is a new
European privacy law (General Data Protection Regulation, GDPR) in place. To
comply with this, various positions and organizations must cooperate smartly to realize Privacy by Design. Through the project, it is examined how Blockchain Organizing can play a role in solving privacy problems, or better in preventing them. The
final goal of PLATO is a blockchain community to bring supply and demand of
different Privacy by Design instruments (courses, advise services, impact analyses
instrument’s etcetera) together. A schematic representation of this network is:

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

169

Figure 5.1: Blockchain community for new privacy law (GDPR)

Projects start with an idea. Subsequently you want to test whether Blockchain Organizing can bring something meaningful to the implementation of your idea, your
innovation. We have developed a quick scan and an extensive questionnaire for this
which are part of our Blockchain Organizing transition program161.
5.2 How do you start and how do you move
Blockchain Organizing is mainly suitable for situations where there are many buyers
and sellers, each with a relatively small market share in a non-transparent market,
with relatively new instruments but without many rules, access thresholds, with intermediaries, high transaction costs and sufficient financing options. In the example
of PLATO there are a number of positions, both with companies as governments,
on the demand side (on the right in figure 5.1) that have to deal with GDPR, for
example: purchasing, sales, HRM, communication, marketing and management. On
the supply side, there are service providers such as advisors, trainers, architects, lawyers, developers and auditors. When we bring all these positions to an individual level
(the smallest building block) there are often dozens of people involved in designing,
developing and implementing solutions to comply with GDPR. All these people
must be managed at minimum transaction costs. You can solve this with the use of
organizational technology and more specific with Blockchain Organizing.

161 See also ‘solutions’ tab on website weconet.org

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

170

Say you have the idea to set up PLATO and you want to test if this idea can be
implemented with the Blockchain Organizing concept. To be able to make a wellfounded choice on what the best way is to start your blockchain project, it is useful
to first answer a few questions and to set criteria. A possible research question is:
Can I use Blockchain Organizing to bring supply and demand of services together at
minimum transaction costs, so an answer can be given to the challenges of GDPR?
Possible sub-questions are:
▪ Describe your project in your own words: what does it entail and what is the role
of your (project) idea in the total (domain: market for ‘Privacy by Design instruments)?
▪ Which problem will be solved by the final result of the project?
▪ What are the main developments in the domain, especially regarding the development and application of organizational technology?
▪ Which instruments (values) are required and offered?
▪ How many buyers and sellers are there in the domain?
▪ Is the domain transparent: do buyers and sellers have similar information at the
same time?
▪ Are you dealing with a local or global market?
▪ Which resistances do you expect? Who are the opponents of your idea and are
there barricades such as laws and access thresholds?
▪ Who are the ambassadors of your idea?
▪ Are there competitors with a similar idea and what is their investment capacity?
▪ Which blockchain concepts do you think you need?
▪ With how many stakeholders do you want to start a project? Which of their interests correspond with yours and which differ?
▪ Who are the intended customer, project leader and blockchain expert?
▪ With which target do you want to start the first project? How do you think that
the dot on the horizon looks like (Z) and how does the first step look like (from
A to B)?
▪ Who wants to participate in your project?
▪ How will you finance the first process (from A to B)?
Development model
A workable model, to create a focus and development plan, after asking and answering these questions and to determine what you will start with, is the model from
Iansiti and Lakhani that appeared in the Harvard Business Review162. Using this
model, you can draw an analogy between ‘e-mail’, as first application of internet technology and ‘Bitcoin’, as first application of blockchain technology. Based on their
experiences, Iansiti and Lakhani have developed a framework for blockchain adaption. We have used this model and adjusted it for Blockchain Organizing that is wider
than just blockchain technology. The framework consists of two axes:

162 See also article in Harvard Business Review by Marco Iansiti and Karim R. Lakhani, both Har-

vard professors: 'The Truth About Blockchain' (January-February 2017).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

171

1. Degree of complexity and coordinating capacity that you require. The more parties are involved in the innovation, the more difficult it becomes to manage this.
It takes longer to reach standardization and consensus, but once there is consensus, you have a greater chance and a greater domain to apply your innovation. The
success of a network organization (also called network effect) often depends on
the number of participating nodes.
2. Degree of novelty of the required technology and organizational model and therefore adaptability for the user. The newer the application, the more effort must be
spent on the adaption (adaptation/adoption?) by a wide audience.
This creates four quadrants that can also represent four development stages. In each
quadrant, there is an example of Blockchain Organizing. The example from the internet development is printed in italics. Developments with a low degree of novelty
and complexity will be embraced first.
High

High

3. Substitution
Loyalties based on Bitcoin
Online bookstore

4. Transformation

Table 5.1: Framework for planning blockchain developments/investments 163

We explain the separate quadrants:
1. Single Use
Like e-mail was the killer application with the development of internet, Bitcoin is the
killer application for blockchain technology. While e-mail was the replacement for
faxes and letters, with all its well-known advantages, Bitcoin is a replacement for
traditional money with immediate, clearly visible, advantages for the user. At the beginning of a technical development there is often only one application for the technology. Think of pumping water from mines with the steam engine. The steam engine only became a success when several applications were developed such as the
steam locomotive and drives in the factory.
2. Localization
With this development, you do apply a new technology, but you do this in your own
environment. Therefore, you are only dealing with a limited number of stakeholder
163 According to the model of Marco Iansiti and Karim R. Lakhani © HBR.ORG.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

172

(parties/departments). At this moment, we especially notice the development at
banks and insurance companies to create an own, private or permissioned blockchain. This is similar to the development of an own communication platform within
companies, based on the TCP/IP protocol. Another comparison is the difference in
the development of internet and intranet applications. Companies have much further
progressed in the transfer of (analogue) processes to their intranet environment, than
they have to internet communities. Examples of these intranet applications are Employee Self Service applications, employees can use to request holidays, choose employee benefits and exchange overtime for salary. This quadrant is mainly related to
the sixth driver for Blockchain Organizing that we described in paragraph 4.1 (Driver
6: process improvement).
3. Substitution
In the third quadrant, technological innovation is not that big, but there are several
parties involved that require coordination. With this transformation we see the transfer of processes to a partnership or platform. There is replacement, in case of Blockchain Organizing, when a limited number of internal processes are transferred to an
external platform while the technology to perform these processes does not have to
change a lot. An example of this is the transfer of processes to a shared service center
that still communicates via e-mail with the different actors on the network.
4. Transformation
There is transformation when processes are transferred to a shared transaction network and these processes are performed using a new technology and a new management model. This means, for example, that checking the execution of an agreement
between a buyer and supplier is no longer done based on random checks by an employee of the quality department. The execution of a transaction and monitoring
thereof is done fully automatically and conditionally via a blockchain protocol.
The risk of failure increases from quadrant 1 up to 4. For example, if you can invest
little and you want to/may be at little risk, you can best start with quadrant 1. Once
this innovation is running smoothly, you continue with quadrant 2, etcetera. A relatively simple and safe way to start with blockchain technology, is adding an additional
process to existing peer-to-peer platforms or online communities, where you can buy
products or services. An example is the market for business courses in the Netherlands, where a number of platforms are used that could be expanded with the provision and registration of the service (purchase) via blockchain technology. After that
the payment could also be made via for example payment in Bitcoin or an own currency. But this relatively simple expansion also requires a careful planning and study
because processes must be performed securely and faultlessly, especially in the payment system. Plus, large companies have definitely already invested a lot in internal
systems for payments, a connected bank book and accounting system. This could
mean that you could probably best start with the medium-sized and small enterprises
and not with the large companies. Anyway, it seems that we are still awaiting for real

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

173

transformational blockchain applications. This will change when we become aware
off our social problems and how organization technology can help to solve these.
How do you move?
According to the aforementioned John Hagel, large scale interventions are usually
initiated by two alternative sources. A senior officer where you work, could read for
example this book and subsequently wants to apply, top-down, Blockchain Organizing in your organization. We often know from our own experience and from management literature, that this usually does not work properly. The second option is the
formation of a team within the company, that develops an initial idea further so it
can be implemented later in the existing operation and structure. The success ratio
of this approach is often also low because the existing organization is involved in the
innovation too late. So, we usually opt for a hybrid form of the following movements:
1. Top-down and bottom-up, whereby we design and develop a new organization
and transfer existing processes to this new organization
2. Short and long term, we also call this a double decision: you solve short term
problems (for example making payment transactions cheaper) and in parallel, with
at least the same budget and invested at the same level, a long-term project (for
example making money redundant as medium of exchange).
3. Exploitation and Exploration, also known as ambidexterity, whereby you combine
walking over a bridge with building the bridge as to speak.

Table 5.2: Framework for how you can move

We will further explain the axes (Level, Horizon and Ambidexterity):
Level: top-down and bottom-up
Blockchain Organizing is building from a new perspective, on new foundations and
with a new strategy. Blockchain Organizing is Greenfield organizing (building a new

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

174

house), without the ballast (legacy) of Brownfield organizing (repairing old house).
This means that especially old institutions like non-transparent markets, industrial
companies, government authority and traditional management will lose part of their
power, their autonomy and interest because they become less relevant. This will happen gradually because more and more processes are transferred from classical institutions to modern network organizations.
You can distinguish two movements with this (extremes on a scale): on the one hand
there is the top-down approach, whereby existing institutions decide to transfer a
part of their processes to a new organization. On the other, there is the bottom-up
movement, whereby portfolio workers will organize trust and work fundamentally
different. Like in the case where self-employed professionals will organize themselves
via communities in the shape of cooperatives. With the use of Blockchain Organizing
individuals are able to develop organizing capacity similar in size to large heavy institutions at much lower management costs.
By combining top-down and bottom-up approaches, a strong foundation is created
for Blockchain Organizing. An example of this hybrid form is a shared transaction
network where portfolio workers can unite themselves to be able to perform large
assignments. It is a network that is joined by both portfolio workers as well as large
organizations. This shared network creates a capacity similar to a large engineering
firm or law firm.
Both movements, top-down and bottom-up, require a different approach, but must
eventually come together and be used from the same strategy. Many existing companies still think from the competition perspective and less from the cooperation perspective. But this image becomes less relevant with Blockchain Organizing. Think of
the aforementioned competition between major soccer teams, it becomes irrelevant
when people stop watching soccer.
With big assignments, portfolio workers think of cooperation with other freelancers
and with small assignments of competition. Whichever way you turn it, we need a
management innovation that better balances cooperation and competition. You cannot renew the existing, without respecting the old. We see, for example, that new
initiatives like Uber and Airbnb are faced by existing legislation and interest organizations. You can fight the old world in court, or you can also take into account in
your design that existing processes must be transferred to your new building and that
you can better realize this step by step by involving the existing world that is openminded in this respect.

Top-down
With the top-down movement, existing institutions are the unit of thinking, analysis
and design (remember the metaphor: a Lego set). From this position, you can

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

175

reorganize processes with the help of partnerships, shared service centers, supply
chains and new concepts like ‘circular economy’. A company will then become the
fixed core or hub in a network of professionals and transfers certain processes to a
flexible shell or partnership. An example of top-down movement is an existing company, that wants to switch to a 6-hour work day with the use of a CSR program164.
The reason behind is that employees can go home at three o’clock because they have
caring tasks at home. The advantage for the company is that employees come to
work the next day well rested and much more productive during the six hours they
work. But to realize a six-hour working day, the company must cooperate with other
organizations in the region including the government. When a company stands alone
in improving productivity, the company will probably think from a competitive perspective: if we become more productive, I am able to produce more with the same
resources or if I continue to produce the same and I can dismiss people. By cooperating, for example under the direction of a council, from a same interest, by keeping
our welfare state affordable, this will succeed sooner.
Bottom-up
The unit of analysis and design with the bottom-up movement is organizing demand
and supply with the individual as unit of thinking, analysis and design (remember the
metaphor: smallest Lego blocks). A group of self-employed accountants can offer a
similar organizing capacity, via a partnership (for example a cooperative), as a large
accounting firm. In theory, this movement means: reorganizing and coordinating
transactions and making these more transparent and better scalable with the use of
partnerships. The transaction costs can be reduced by using organizational technology.
To join this movement, existing markets must become more transparent and cheaper
and existing companies will have to change their way of thinking, their strategy and
structure. In case of tenders they should give cooperatives the same chance as large
service providers. There is especially an important role here for governments, when
they outsource services. Because the government in particular wants to avoid risks
and wants to reduce insecurities as much as possible, there is an opportunity here for
innovation. When cooperatives show that they can do the same work, with the same
quality or even better, but at much lower costs, there is an opportunity. New organizations, for example, will be created because portfolio workers, with the help of a
shared transaction network, can achieve a similar organizing capacity as large companies, but at much lower transaction costs and without increasing the complexity.
Hybrid
An example of a hybrid form is demonstrated by Constantijn van Oranje-Nassau,
the youngest son of princes Beatrix of the Netherlands and brother of King WillemAlexander. After his law study in Leiden, he followed an internship at the cabinet of
164 Corporate Social Responsibility Program.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

176

the Commissioner of the European Commission. Then he followed a MBA course
at INSEAD, worked for RAND cooperation and became Head of Cabinet of European Commissioner (Digital Agenda) till the end of 2014. According to an interview
in Elsevier Magazine165 he could have continued working at the European Commission, but he was spending eighty percent of his time with people who did not want
to change and who benefitted from a status quo. Member states do not want to move
and neither do established organizations with vested interests.
From the Digital Agenda, he tried to get companies and governments interested, but
according to him this was: ‘moaning and dragging’. This top-down way of working,
did not work for him. He prefers to work with people who like to get things done.
Therefore, he started his own company after his time in Brussels. Now he is, among
other things, Special Envoy for StartupDelta166. He also works on the implementation of the Digital Agenda, but now bottom-up, in combination with his business
and government contacts.
A hybrid movement is a movement whereby you bring a top-down movement and
a bottom-up movement together. This means that the stakeholders in your project
are large companies, entrepreneurs and portfolio workers who want to unite themselves. Large companies see that they can develop a function as hub in a network
organization and portfolio workers need assignments from companies. Traditionally,
a large company could opt for an innovation by either use their own R&D department or purchase the innovation by acquiring a start-up. In both cases little will
change fundamentally because the management model is not renewed. Innovation is
still done with the existing unit of thinking, analysis and design. A fundamental innovation is created when large companies start fundamentally reviewing their management invention of hundred years ago and start replacing their bureaucracy with
organizational technology.
A second movement that you can make in your blockchain project, but then via
another axis (see table 5.2) is the combination of a short and long-term project. When
we look at the PLATO example, large companies should rely more on Privacy by
Design in the long term, instead of using bandages in the short term with more IT
and more procedures. On the supply side professionals should unite to be able to
deliver a customized product to clients through cooperating smarter at lower costs.
Cooperation is required between the different specializations such as an organizational expert, an IT architect and developer, lawyer and auditor. Specialization should
not lead to more complexity and bureaucracy. Therefore, coordination receives at
least the same attention within the PLATO project as specialization and finalization.
Horizon: short and long term

165 Article in Elsevier magazine by Carla Joosten and Jean Dohmen (31-12-2016, Dutch only).
166 Accelerator for Dutch start-ups.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

177

Many organizations that we advise, ask for solutions for short term problems or how
to apply blockchain technology in order to make internal processes more efficient.
They hear about the disruptive character of the technology and want to do something
with this new technology without understanding what is really is. They do not want
to miss out167, but they are not thinking about a long-term vision or strategy. Sometimes they hear that blockchain is aimed at making existing institutions redundant,
but that will not apply for the own company, they think: My position and my company will continue to exist, as long as I embrace new technology on time.
But this, often linear adjustment, is not enough. It is no longer just about technology.
It is also about a fundamental management innovation. It is about the contribution
of your organization in solving organizational and societal problems. When you only
look at Blockchain Organizing from a short-term self-interest, your investment will
probably fail. The paradox is that you must first dare to let go of your own position
and organization in order to continue to exist. This requires courage and a shortterm and long-term approach, preferably combined in one program or strategy.
Therefore, we use the double track decision (hybrid form) more and more at the
organizations that we advise.
An example of a double decision was The NATO Double-Track Decision. It was a
decision of NATO (1979), to offer the Warsaw Pact a mutual limitation of mediumrange ballistic missiles and intermediate-range ballistic missiles combined with the
threat that in case of disagreement NATO would deploy more middle-range nuclear
weapons in Western Europe. This reciprocity turned out to be successful in the end.
Short-term changes will often be organized from a top-down movement and longterm changes (actual innovation), will be organized more bottom-up and with external pressure, including technology.
A double track decision combines both into a hybrid form. You could see a double
track decision as building a bridge (long-term) while you walk across (short-term). A
double track decision is a strategy to combine practice (short-term) and principle
(long-term) into something new. This strategy ensures that short-term problems are
solved within existing companies and markets, under the condition that a long-term
project is also started at the same time with a similar budget and invested at the same
level. The long-term project is regularly tested and no resources may be transferred
from the long-term to the short-term project.
Ambidexterity: exploitation and exploration
The word ambidexterity means that you can use both right and left hand equally. In
management science it means that you can combine exploitation (management) with
exploration (innovation). Organizational ambidexterity needs leadership that sees
that both exploitation and innovation are important. They should be in balance and
need diversity in the people and resources. The one cannot do without the other.
167 Also called Fear of Missing out (FOMO).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

178

You cannot innovate without revenues, unless you collect the necessary resources
from outside the organization but that gives you other challenges such as keeping
shareholders satisfied the next quarter. And probably, without innovation revenues
will stop in the future.
So ambidexterity has also to do with short and long term, but innovation is not always important only for the long term. Innovation could help your organization to
stay restless, young and challenged at the short term. Mostly innovation gives people
new energy, but it could also frighten them to lose their job for instance. You should
give attention to both sides of the effects of innovation.
The question is not whether or not you should improve existing business or disrupt,
the question is how you combine these two and whether or not you do execute it
within the same fiscal legal entity for example. You can imagine that you do business
as usual in your existing company and parallel you innovate via a new organization
(form). You cannot stay in business without changing and adapting, but that could
be changing within the system: Brownfield organizing, operational excellence, improving the candle or sustainable innovation. Most managers are optimizing the existing system with a mindset of structuring, Lean Six Sigma, Agile or Self Organizing
Teams for instance. But what if the existing system is the problem?
For changing the system (from candle to light bulb) you need disruptive innovation,
you need Greenfield organizing and Blockchain Organizing at the moment is an example of the latter. But Blockchain Organizing goes further than what a lot of people
mean with exploration or even further than disruptive innovation. Most businesses
keep their own organization in the middle, between purchase and sales or collecting
and distributing and they think of innovating new products, services, and processes
but still organized with or from their existing organization. But as we learn from this
book: blockchain start with giving up some autonomy. Your organizing is not the
center of the business universe, but just a part of a whole. Without changing your
worldview, blockchain will not work the way it was designed for.
5.3 Project-related approach
As we stated before, you can come up with many application that can work with
blockchain technology: from maintaining your e-portfolio or patient file, to selling/buying your house. Everything with value, the ownership of which can be transferred (digitally) from seller to buyer, can in principle be registered on a blockchain.
Plus, according to the Blockchain Organizing concept the technology is not primarily
important, but the organizational problem that you want to solve and the organization model you use for that. This means that Blockchain Organizing does not primarily focus on the improvement of traditional management, but on reinventing the
management itself. It is based on Innovation by Design. This concept assumes that
you can better start a new organization and transfer existing processes to this new
organization. You’ll have less problems with legacies, traditional management and

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

179

old IT systems. When we, apart from improving processes, look at the other drivers
for Blockchain Organizing (purpose, productivity, privacy, propaganda and power),
they can only be solved in a structural way by going back to the drawing board.
Innovation by Design means that you bring a number of elements together, as described in this book. Some examples are:
▪ what is your perspective, from which one do you look at the future?
▪ what is your wide and deep analysis of the current management concepts
▪ which management problems do you want to solve?
▪ which (technical) concept will you use for this?
▪ which organizational and management principles will you use?
▪ how are you going to fill in the ten core connections?
▪ what does your innovation program looks like?
The last question deals with the process that must lead to your management innovation and the use of Blockchain Organizing. A program includes various (sub) projects
that you plan to get from A to Z. Therein you must always indicate how your project
and eventually your innovation will distinguish itself (focus, branding) from the rest.
What is your target and target group (the stakeholders), who will be the client, project
leader and the organizational expert? When you have completed this, you can define
(in a ‘SMART’ way) the first step (from A to B).
When you want to start with Blockchain Organizing within your organization, supply
chain or ecosystem, it is important (as blockchain lead) to develop a vision. Subsequently, you can share this vision with other stakeholders, for example in a workshop.
A workshop ‘Blockchain by Design’ can be held based on a vision document. This
vision document contains a perspective, problem and solution direction. This document can be elaborated on by the client, blockchain lead (project leader) or for example a blockchain consultant. A vision includes a number of insights such as: ‘Technology solves little if we do not first look at the organizational design’. During the
workshop, the functional needs, available concepts and pre-conditions can also be
sketched for the first time.
Participants in the meeting are asked to respond to the vision document. Preferably,
the participants in the workshop should be stakeholders in your project. The developed vision document will be discussed during this workshop based on the (preintroduced) perspectives of the participants. Subsequently, vision, needs, concepts
and pre-conditions will be included in your project plan for the implementation of
Blockchain by Design. If desired by the client, participants in the workshop can subsequently be invited to participate in your blockchain project. To reduce resistances
and to increase the success rate, the workshop should have a high interactive content
and mainly focus on the widest possible supported commitment for the start of your
project. The basis of interaction should not be the discussion, but the guided dialogue. By asking open and non-suggestive questions you could together determine
what the old conditionings of the traditional management are and discover new

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

180

perspectives. During the workshop, the various stakeholders are separately and
jointly challenged to arrive at an open innovation, to take opportunities and to solve
problems.
Example workshop program
An example program of your workshop could be:
1. An introduction to the problems or the challenges of the project that you want to
start, seen from the perspective of the relevant domain and from the project initiator/client. Questions that will be discussed could include:
a. What can new management concepts, like Blockchain Organizing, mean
for solving the problem?
b. How can we cooperate smarter between business, government,
knowledge institutions and communities?
c. What are the principles and foundations of New Organizing, management innovation and Blockchain by Design?
d. How can we better solve our problem based on these principles?
2. A series of presentations in which the various participants present their own perspective on one or more aspects from your vision, idea or problem. Plus, they
share their ideas regarding some directions to arrive at an imagined destination.
These insights are sent to the project leader in advance.
3. A number of round table discussions in small groups. Ideas about problem definition, functional needs, technologies, management models, pre-conditions and a
first project assignment are exchanged and determined here.
4. Finally, the results of the discussions are summarized and bundled and will serve
as input for the next step. This creates a chain of progress and the actual start-up
of a Blockchain by Design project.
Blockchain by Design is not change, but renewal
As mentioned before, Blockchain by Design is: Greenfield organizing instead of
Brownfield organizing. It is innovation instead of change. Despite the fact that a
billion dollar industry has developed around change management and change projects, very little research has been done into the effectiveness of all these projects.
The first important question is already a difficult one: how do you measure the success of a change project? When exactly is a project successful? Many ideas circulate
around the success of change projects, but these myths are not really (scientifically)
verifiable. One figure that is being tossed around is that seventy percent of change
projects fail. It has also been said that you need charismatic leaders for changes that
self-steering teams would perform better and that early participation of stakeholders
would lead to less resistance. What is not a myth is that every failure should be better
in the process. Your blockchain project will probably follow the next steps:

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

181

Figure 5.2 Next time: fail better

The success of a project could be measured by the percentage of transactions that
are no longer purely done by yourself or via outsourcing, but within new entities such
as a blockchain community. This number is currently still very low, but when measured in a verifiable way it could better assess the development of Blockchain Organizing. In the long-term, this number could indicate whether these projects contribute
to the improvement of the productivity and the creation of more surplus time.
So far, the beginning of your Blockchain by Design project. Now we continue with
a more detailed look at the project approach that we have used often with a number
of our blockchain projects.
Starting-up a project yourself
You can start a project yourself. It does not matter whether you have your own business or for example work at a large organization. With our working method, we focus
on enterprising people who want to solve organizational problems with Blockchain
Organizing such as the low productivity growth and the new privacy law. The projects that we guide as consultants mainly focus on using organizational technology,
to become more productive or to better assure privacy and security. Projects take
place in knowledge-intensive domains with many buyers and sellers. A project must
eventually lead to the exploitation of a shared transaction network, with which you
can offer, order, administratively settle and digitally pay products/services/data requests. The aforementioned PLATO project must lead eventually to a situation
whereby a company can quickly assemble the right building blocks and connect these
at low transaction costs to enable Privacy by Design.
To quickly test whether your ideas can be organized with blockchain, we have developed a quick scan method with ten questions. In addition, there is also an extensive
questionnaire once you are further progressed into your project. After the quick scan
the next ‘test of the quality of your idea’ could be a kick-off. Try to get ten to twenty
stakeholders interested in your idea. Try to ‘tempt’ them to come to your meeting
and provide an active contribution without them knowing exactly what they will

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

182

receive in return. During the first meeting, you want to test your initial idea with the
people present as much as possible. This means that you are open to additions and
criticism from the participants. On the one hand, it is advisable to first explain your
idea in general terms, so there is room for the participants to make modifications.
On the other hand, your story must not be too abstract and vague because people
will then drop out because it is not concrete enough.
When participants wonder after the kick-off whether they should spend their valuable time on your project, you must make a choice: continue to try to make people
enthusiastic or to draw the conclusion that you do not fit together. It is also possible
that your idea is good, but that the timing in not right. The trick is to find a good
balance between open and structured. A nice dialogue form for this is for example
Openspace, Roundtable or ThoughtExpedition.
Anyway, you must already have a well thought-out idea of what will be your target
and target group of the first (sub) project. These details can be made more precise
by points brought up by the participants. When you continue with your own idea,
without using the feedback, it is possible that participants drop out. It is also possible
that you encourage other participants with your clear and focused idea, to join. Between the first and second meeting you have individual contacts with the participants
to evaluate and to ask whether they want to continue or not, and if so in which role.
Based on this evaluation you tighten the target and target group. After the second
meeting, you must ensure that each participant has a similar understanding level of
your idea, your domain and theme. Anyway, the target of your project must be clear
at the second meeting.
Blockchain project goal
A project can have different goals. These can also be different stages of your transition program, for example:
1. Start: exploring each other’s values, perspective, target, visions and ambitions.
Choose project leader, expert, fill in quick scan, prepare kick-off etcetera.
2. Awareness: why do we need Blockchain Organizing for our organization or our
domain? What problem do we solve?
3. Proof of concept: setting up a test/trial organization and showing that blockchain
works with a simple, but workable demo.
4. Setting up and conducting a feasibility study that must give a definite answer
within a certain time whether it is feasible to use Blockchain Organizing (model,
infrastructure and program) in the researched domain. You can research the technical feasibility but also the market opportunities.
5. Setting up and implementing an open-innovation project, that must define the
functional needs, wishes and pre-conditions within a certain time frame, for an
organizational model, infrastructure and transition program for domain.
6. Setting up and implementing a development process that develops an organizational model, knowledge structure and transition program for the domain, based
on functional wishes, needs and pre-conditions.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

183

7. Setting up and implementing a (new) organizational entity (for example foundation, limited company or cooperative), which can exploit a blockchain community
within a certain time.
The main target of setting up projects is the development and eventual exploitation
of a shared transaction network for your organization within a certain domain. A
shared transaction network is a network with many sellers and many buyers (n:n
community). With this network you can, almost fully digitally and at minimum transaction costs, profile, select, purchase and pay products/services. Using organizational
technology helps with this. Plus, technology is the coordinating capacity between
supply and demand (see also figure 1.3). The underlying infrastructure (general data
utility facility) for blockchain communities is in principle the same for any community. The differences are mainly in the focus (target -and target group-oriented communication) and could be programmed with script language.
In addition to a clear target for your project you must also define a clear target group
for your project. On the one hand, there is a target group with which you want to
implement your projects (also called project stakeholders), and on the other hand
you have the target group (your customers) that benefit from the result of your project.
Target group project
Once you have a project plan ready and you want to start with your blockchain project, the choice of your project members, your project stakeholders, is important.
These stakeholders can differ from the participants in your first kick-off. It can be
possible, for example, that there are mainly experts present during the first kick-off
and then each expert will approach a number of market parties to participate in the
project. In the beginning, you must especially trust experts and communicate clearly
which market parties you are looking for. Subsequently you must trust the experts
that they will find the right parties. Usually we advise a project group of ten to twenty
stakeholders and divide these equally into for example:
▪ Market parties: this group can usually be sub-divided into:
o Primary market parties, those who will later on be seller or buyer in a
transaction in the community;
o Secondary market parties, those who offer products and services with the
exploitation of the community to enable or facilitate transactions: hosting
company, consultancy or training agencies, legal advice, software developer, digital currency platform and intermediaries.
▪ Project suppliers: only present in project: project leader, organizational expert,
blockchain consultant, research agency, media partner, IT architect, software developer, tester, hosting company for Proof of Concept.
You will notice that as soon as your idea gets traction, there will also be resistance
from existing parties that feel threatened. That is why it makes sense to take into

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

184

account from the beginning that there will be resistance. A good principle for this is:
‘hope for the best, prepare for the worst’.
5.4 Dealing with resistances
How can you prevent that resistances occur that slow down your blockchain project?
In this book we have discussed the rise of destructive populism that is partly caused
by fear and helplessness of people. When we continuously change things but eventually have the feeling that we gain little from it, we will become apathetic in the next
project and perhaps oppose it. In professional literature, this is called learned helplessness. Learned helplessness is a characteristic of people which can occur when the
desired result is not achieved after a number of attempts to avoid an unfavorable
situation. When attempts to change continue to fail, you eventually become apathetic
and you not even try it the next time, although there is a (new) chance of succeeding.
We have become powerless to escape an unfavorable situation. So, learned helplessness describes a situation whereby people become apathetic as a result of futile attempts to escape the situation. A good example is traffic jams. When you want to
avoid a traffic jam and take another route, but time and time again this route is not
faster, you will eventually not even try to take another route. You undergo the situation although there is another chance that the other route is indeed quicker.
This behavior is substantiated with scientific research by the American psychologist
Martin Seligman168, among others. He conducted extensive research into the phenomenon of learned helplessness. According to Seligman, learned helplessness is
learned during the exposure to uncontrollable events. These are events whereby it is
impossible to respond to a negative stimulus. Seligman performs experiments with
dogs where he puts the floor of the dog house under a weak electric current. Certain
dogs seem to be able to jump over the fence to avoid the expected electric shock.
Other dogs ‘passively’ undergo the pain of the electric shock because they cannot
escape. When these dogs do get the opportunity to escape they do not do this. Apparently, they have developed learned helplessness. They have become apathetic.
With man we see learned helplessness with change processes within companies. They
are tired of change and expect that the project will not change their situation. So,
when a project is started with new opportunities with which they can indeed change
their situation, they probably will not join. They undergo it, but do not participate
actively.
Blockchain Organizing will only succeed when we manage to reinvent the latest management innovation of Taylor and Ford and adjust our perspective on the organization of work and welfare. We must convince people that we can easily switch to a 6hour working day for those who are interested. There will be resistance from the
168 Martin E. P. Seligman (1942-), is an American psychologist, author of self-help books and

professor at the University of Pennsylvania. He conducts research into learned helplessness in
humans (and animals).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

185

‘old-world’ that is still convinced that the firm, the bank, the government are the
right tools to coordinate supply and demand. Most managers will say: If organizational technology ensures that we finish the work sooner, I will look for additional
tasks or I cut the budget of that department. We must dare to let go of this expansion
and old budget thinking. We must also let go of the thought that working hard is
good. When working hard means that we buy products that we do not require, then
working hard is not right169.
From resistance to awareness
Blockchain Organizing is still mainly in the awareness stage and this will be followed
by the design and development stage. After that, Blockchain Organizing will be
brought into reality and applied more and more. How do we deal with this innovation, which resistances will arise and how can we cooperate smartly with the existing
and conditioned world? How can we make sure that the improvement of the productivity indeed leads to a six-hour working day for example. Then it is not useful to
immediately ‘import’ a new technology into an old organizational structure, without
thinking about the meaning of work and unemployment.
As to speak with Plato again: with the body you should also look after the soul. It is
better to first work on ‘awareness’, to look at more fundamental questions and for
example learn from the internet development and the consequences thereof on the
organization of work. In the first stage, it is useful to become aware together with
the stakeholders and to find answers to questions like:
▪ What is progress? When will our children have a better life?
▪ Can we first think about the questions what we will do with the surplus time when
technology makes us more productive?
▪ What will be the role of companies in shared transaction networks? Will Blockchain Organizing disrupt existing organizational forms and organizations or can
they adapt quickly enough to, for example, a hub in a network?
▪ Are directors, politicians and policymakers aware that internet has fundamentally
changed little in the way we organize supply and demand? What will we learn from
this regarding the development and application of blockchain?
▪ At what rate will the innovation (have to) take place? Which drivers are there and
what is the urgency of the new privacy law for example?
▪ To what extent will blockchains become interoperable, mutable or replaceable?
Will we mainly start with private blockchains or will blockchain be used more in
communities?
▪ Will existing companies make disinvestments in existing systems such as CRM,
HRM, ERP, purchase and payment systems to be able to transfer processes to
blockchain communities?

169 See for example article by Mark Whitehouse in The Wall Street Journal: 'Number of the Week:

Americans Buy More Stuff They Don’t Need', Americans spend 1,200 billion dollars per year on
things they do not need. (23-4-2011).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

186

▪ How quickly will education, consultancy firms, business administration and managements training courses and training agencies switch to new insights related to
organizing trust, work and economy?
▪ Which sectors or countries will be the first to take on Blockchain Organizing?
Where is the most low-hanging fruit?
▪ How quickly will the legislator follow?
▪ How quickly will software developers take on new technologies and train themselves for this? What will be the supply of blockchain developers later on?
When you get insight into the answers to these questions you will be able to better
estimate resistances within and outside your organization. When you complete the
project group, you can for example start with research. This can be a feasibility study,
but also a design assignment. In all cases it is wise to perform research, design, innovation and development process in a structured way. Of course, the design itself does
not have to be done in a structured way, iteration and creativity, play an important
role in this.
We do not know exactly how long it will take before blockchain has sufficient applications and volume, so people will consider it as ‘the new normal’. When we take
into account how for example the TCP/IP protocol has developed itself in three
decades, we expect that a major breakthrough of blockchain technology and therefore Blockchain Organizing will take one to two decades. Our society is some kind
of super tanker that does not suddenly change direction. Still it is useful to already
start today with the development and application of ideas and innovations surrounding Blockchain Organizing.
The main lesson of ‘large scale interventions’ is to start as early as possible with controllable learning. According to the insights of John Hagel, you need to track down
opponents of innovation (resistors of change), weaken them and strengthen supporters (ambassadors of change), as early as possible. For example, you can strengthen
supporters with a good workable and useful Proof of Concept (POC) application.
This means: taking acceptable risks to prepare people and organization step by step
for what is to come.
According to Hagel, we must be careful that we not just see large scale interventions
as a rational process with which you move towards the final target, step by step.
According to Hagel, it is mainly a political process170. We agree with this. Since the
beginning of the nineties we have experience with the use of internet technology, for
