example with the smart organization of supply and demand in the SCM, HR and
government domain. It is our experience that fundamental innovation cannot just be
realized by using a new technology, not even in combination with a new management

170 See also video of Singularity University: ‘How to Become a Champignon of Change’ (14-11-

2014).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

187

model. There must also be leadership and problems to be solved. According to us,
that is now even more relevant than in the 1990s.
Furthermore, there are also other forces that play a role, such as dis-rationality171.
Often you are not able yet to reason or rationalize a choice, whether to embrace a
system or not. The innovation is still too abstract. It differs too much from what you
are used to. Abstract is not the same as complex. Abstract means omitting what is
not relevant. We ‘reject’ a simple world because we do not know yet what we must do with the
surplus time. Plus, many choices are made emotionally, instead of rationally and are
often politically driven. We assess, often implicitly, an innovation on the risk of losing
our power base.
In addition, with the rise of a technology many people do not take the time to understand it. For most people, and rightly so, technology must be easy to use. We want
to experience technology as something self-evident, something that works for us and
that is how it should be. Many people do not have the time to delve into it or think
that they will not understand it anyway. Maybe they are also afraid to discover something that can jeopardize their current reality. However, when you delve into it too
late, in this case Blockchain Organizing, the result may be that you are too late with
learning, with innovating and adjusting yourself to a new order.
John Hagel sees three important parts of large change processes, or as we call them:
innovation processes:
1. Identify and neutralize the enemies of innovation. This sounds easier than it is,
because during a project meeting or kick-off, for example, nobody will be against
renewal or innovation. But if this innovation undermines their own position, people in the comfort zone will start to oppose from their own position or own organization without this being noticed by the project group.
2. Identify the supporters of innovation and give them as much as possible support
with the process of: developing awareness, feasibility studies, open-innovation,
development and implementation.
3. Before you start implementing an innovation you must make sure that the opponents are sufficiently weakened, and the supporters sufficiently strengthened, so
it is evident to implement the innovation.
If you, after reading this book, have become interested in using Blockchain Organizing within your own organization or ecosystem, it is important to know that there
are solutions for problems that you will probably face. However, these are often not
practical, short term solutions that usually make the problem worse anyhow, but
fundamental solutions that will last longer. From own experience we know that the
further development and application of disruptive innovations can encounter a lot
of resistance. You have to imagine that most positions and organizations that
171 Characteristic, that we are often unable to make rational decisions despite our intelligence.

This is because we are conditioned.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

188

currently exist, have been created to reduce insecurity. We have started to globalize
demand and supply more and more, which increases the need for insecurity reduction. We find it normal that there are purchase and sales conditions, while we forget
to coordinate these.
Due to efficiency pressure, we have started to use more and more specialists. When
these are not properly coordinated, the complexity increases and therefore also insecurity and inefficiency in supply-chains. Subsequently we are conditioned in the solutions we come up with. We solve new problems with the same frame of mind from
which they originated. We find it normal that we use money to trade while this was
not the case in most of our human existence. We find it normal that we organize
work with companies, while the company, in the form as we know it currently, is
only a few hundred years old.
In Western Europe we find it normal that there is a government that is responsible
for our life, from when we are born till we die, while we forget that more than hundred years ago almost everything was organized privately. And some people believe
that we are entitled to welfare while they forget that you must become more productive if you want more welfare. All conditionings that we find difficult to get lose.
New starts by letting go of the old
When you ask someone: What is hard to forget, the answer usually is: cycling, skiing
or driving a car. When you learn how to cycle for example, you build up reflexes.
These are some kind of conditionings. They have become a kind of permanent paths
in your brain. The advantage is that you do not have to think when you cycle in a
busy town. The disadvantage is that you find it difficult to get rid of these conditionings. Well-known is the YouTube video ‘The Backwards Brain Bicycle - Smarter Every
Day’ where Destin Sandlin, an engineer from the US, has a bike made with two gears
between the bicycle handlebar and the wheel. When you steer the bike to the left, the
bike goes to the right. It seems difficult to learn how to cycle on this new bike. Not
because cycling is difficult to learn, but we find it difficult to reduce existing reflexes
and to let go off conditionings. It is no surprise that it is easier for children to learn
how to cycle on the reversed bike. Innovation therefore starts with letting go of the
old. This is difficult, but indeed necessary. So, if you want a disruptive management
invention, like Blockchain Organizing, to be a success, you must actively start with
letting go of old conditionings. Luckily there are all kinds of tools for this.
So, there is a way of thinking, a way of organizing with a new technology that enables
us to organize trust, without increasing the complexity. That alone is revolutionary,
because in the past centuries the complexity increased more and more when organizing trust. But even this revolutionary ‘invention’ will only succeed if we first and
foremost address the question how we can usefully spend surplus time that arises
when we smartly use organizational technology to reduce the number of intermediaries.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

189

If we use management technology without considering what we will do with the surplus time, we, as human beings, will not benefit much from the use of technology.
When we reassure people with the idea that robotics is also creating new jobs, this is
a form of self-imposed immaturity. When we do not think about a meaningful interpretation for the intermediaries that are made redundant, these intermediaries will
continue to oppose innovation and find other ways to preserve work. When we do
not learn that internet has not made us more productive or democratic, blockchain
will also change little. It is up to our generation to proof that we are smart and have
learned from the past.
Innovations do not work with full employment
The main problem, according to us, is the contradictory policy of both innovation
and full employment. The meaning that we give to (un)employment and the way we
organize trust plays a crucial part in this whole story. Managers (and directors and
supervisors in general) also play an important role in this. Therefore, our attention
to the theme: Blockchain Organizing for managers. The most resistance to innovations occurs because we find it difficult to let go of the old conditioning.
The idea that working hard is good, that entrepreneurs must grow and that we need
full employment is framed. Innovations will only work if we identify ourselves less
with the existing world including economized work. We innovate more and more
(computer) machines to do the work for us. It is time that we, as human beings,
contemplate what we will do with the time that is given to us otherwise the system
that brought us so much prosperity and helped us to pursuit happiness, will, eventually make us very unhappy.
Conclusion
As mentioned in the preface, many people do not see management as an invention.
They probably also have a different view on management innovation. They cannot
form a clear picture for themselves of a disruptive innovation such as replacing traditional management with a protocol, similar to replacing the candle with the light
bulb. The light bulb was not created by improving the candle step by step, but
through pioneering, through the presence of certain materials (like tungsten) and
through other inventions like electricity. The invention of the light bulb, same as the
invention of a blockchain, cannot be assigned to one person, but it is a process of
trial and error. Plus, not just one aspect, like technology, is important, but also for
example successfully convincing people to use the product. One of the light bulb
pioneers was Royal Philips, a Dutch technology company headquartered in Amsterdam, probably became successful with the light bulb through the cooperation between Gerard Philips (engineer) and his brother Anton (the entrepreneur) and their
father Frederik who acted as financer.
Much later, the invention of the light bulb led to other applications, such as the electron tube that is used in televisions and later computer monitors. With the development of Blockchain Organizing there will be more aspects that will be decisive, and

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

190

the invention will lead to a large number of applications and new inventions. However, the time must be right for this. Technology must solve social problems and
various positions will have to cooperate smartly to achieve the target. But the main
thing is that you dare to let go of the old establishment. If you still have candles in
stock for the next ten years, you will not quickly switch to the light bulb, even though
it has been proven that it is better and cheaper.
Asking the right questions is essential in this. Philosophy, curiosity, research and education play an important role in the transition phase we are in. In the past we saw
that Sophia (the Greek meaning for insight and wisdom) is more important in fundamental transitions, than Techne (art and science). But many politicians, economists,
managers and education institutions still focus on the latter with the current transition. When it concerns blockchain, they are very busy preparing managers and also
tax specialists, lawyers, accountants, HR professionals and administrative staff and
with training them for other work within the existing organizational form and the
associated compartmentalization. They say: Robotization is not bad, because it creates new jobs. However, these advocates of employment ignore the intention of innovation and the use of tools for the benefit of people.
They mainly think of the contribution of innovation to the economic growth. They
think linearly and are insufficiently curious about a different meaning of work and
unemployment. It is time to let go of dogma for a new vision, or as formulated by
Abraham Lincoln: “The dogmas of the quiet past are inadequate to the stormy present. The
occasion is piled high with difficulty, and we must rise with the occasion. As our case is new, so we
must think anew and act anew.”
So far, some insights regarding (project-related) innovating and developing Blockchain Organizing for your organization of network. We finish this book with a look
at the future of Blockchain Organizing.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

191

6. The future of Blockchain Organizing
For those of you who are not yet aware of it: our society is squeaking and creaking
and has started to move on the surface with a strong undercurrent. There is anger,
insecurity and a sense of injustice that affects many people more than the lack of
economic growth. The old establishment has become too unstable and will have to
be replaced. What could be the underlying causes (and similarities) of Trump, Brexit,
fake news, populism, propaganda, climate change, immigration, increasing inequality,
debts, problems in healthcare, education and security? And what could Blockchain
Organizing do about it, you might wonder? Not everyone will immediately see a
connection between a management innovation and solving all kinds of social problems. However, in this book we show, from a new perspective, that digital transformation and blockchain technology, in combination with a new organizational model,
can actually help solve organizational problems and therefore social problems. Because we will become more productive in offices, for example, we have more time
available to at least think about social problems. Because who has got time lately to
read background articles for elections for example? A democracy presupposes that
we have time to inform us before we take a decision.
The core of our argument is that we are in a transition of an establishment that was
aimed at ‘creating’ more and more welfare, to a system that is aimed at controlling what
we have. Organizing a sustainable welfare mainly costs time and attention. In order
to create time and attention we must especially become more productive in offices.
Blockchain Organizing can achieve this, provided it is applied correctly. Blockchain,
and tackling the unrest and insecurity that we experience in our community, has one
main similarity: organizing and managing trust. The unrest that we experience, especially since the economic crisis in 2008, is all about trust and uncertainty and the way
we organize these. Obviously, there are people, when given the opportunity and a
motive, cannot be trusted. So, we need tools to organize trust. Trust is the basis of
work, trade and economy and therefore for our society. But trust arrives by foot and
leaves by horse. It is important to take a thorough look at this and to ask ourselves:
what is the best way to organize trust, also for the future. The future is where our
children will live in. In this final chapter, we take a look at the future of Blockchain
Organizing. The near and far future, but we start with distinguishing a number of
stages in the development of blockchain that we can already observe.
6.1 Blockchain power
If we take a look at the distribution of power in the Bitcoin blockchain172, we can
conclude that the old law of sociologist Robert Michels173 also applies to this situation. The ‘iron’ law of the oligarchy states that all organizational forms, irrespective
172 For example: the centralization of mining pools and distribution of bitcoins ownership.
173 See also the Weconomics theory book, paragraph 2.3: ‘Organization basic forms’.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

192

of the democratic or decentralized governance structure in the beginning, will inevitably become oligarchic. The cause of this law is the trend among the rulers to organize around more or less natural behavior of our species:
1. Defend own interests
2. Gratitude of the guided group
3. Passivity of the masses
Michels observes that directors, in the bureaucratic model of his mentor Max Weber,
gain more and more power and can also maintain this, because they have more resources and information as a result of their position. They also have power over
other people's careers, so they will always choose directors who will strengthen their
own position and will not overthrow them.
Another consequence of this concentration of power is the unwillingness to change.
The status quo is the foundation of power. As a result, those who have the power to
change have every interest in changing as less as possible. The present situation is
the basis for their power. Michels came to this conclusion from his practical experience that every group of rulers such as factory directors, politicians, church-leaders
but also labor unions, has a tendency to evolve into an oligarchy.
Democratically elected rulers will often find themselves in a small group of their own
kind, ‘yes-man’ and co-rulers and lose contact with the people that have voted for
them and put them in charge. By studying political parties Michels found out that the
problem lies in the nature of humans and the way they organize trust and work via
entities with a collective goal. In a modern democracy organizations emerge, and
with the traditional (and natural) growth DNA, they will become more complex, become paradoxically less democratic with a higher need for central governance, unless
we find a way to organize trust without increasing complexity.
There comes a time when every growing organization is confronted with coordination and ordering issues that until now could only be solved with more bureaucracy.
The question in our new context of globalization, digitalization and sustainability is:
will this coordinating mechanism be a social bureaucracy (social script) or technical
bureaucracy (technical script such as a blockchain protocol). To make a bureaucracy
efficient in traditional organizations, with little distinction between mechanic and organic organizing, a hierarchy was needed. Daily decisions cannot be taken by large
groups of people. This means that the power in the organization comes into the
hands of a small group or sometimes one person: the CEO with the danger of a
single point of failure. In addition, the CEO, or small group of rulers, will do everything in their power to maintain their power and expand it to protect it.
Conversely, the most autocratic ruler also needs other people in and outside the organization. Power is made possible by the (initial) gratitude of the group that is led
and the passivity of the environment in which 'the power' can develop. This makes
it possible for the ruler to defend his or her own interest. In order to prevent this,

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

193

transparency is required and a policy, strategy and tools to prevent concentration of
power. According to the iron law of Michels, the final result of an organization is
that eventually every organization develops in an oligarchic direction. Maybe it is
time that Blockchain Organizing is going to challenge this law with a good consensus
mechanism, transparency, low cost decision-making and democracy on a decentralized network for and from the people. This sound unrealistic, but is it as unrealistic
as having the idea in 1969 to operate Spotify one day on the internet?
6.2 Blockchain stages
In an article in the Harvard Business Review174, Vinay Gupta175 discusses a number
of important stages that blockchain has undergone. According to him, we are in the
middle of a ‘silent’ revolution that has taken place in the last ten years. We have
expanded his overview in certain areas:
1. The first important innovation is Bitcoin176, currently used by millions of people.
Bitcoin started with the publication of white papers by Nakamoto, but continues
to build on previous technologies, such as cryptography, database technology, the
TCP/IP protocol and distributed systems.
2. The second important innovation is called blockchain, an underlying technology
of Bitcoin. By separating the technology (blockchain) from the application
(Bitcoin), an enormous potential of new innovations is created, such as smart contracts, identity management, document control and other applications outside digital currencies. Currently, almost every large financial or government institution is
investigating the possibilities of blockchain technology. In 2017, about fifteen percent of the banks, expects to actually use this technology with the execution of
their transactions177.
3. According to Gupta, the third innovation is: smart contracts. These smart contracts are made in a next generation technology of which Ethereum is the first and
most important development platform at the moment. Ethereum is a kind of (development) platform with hundreds of different projects that are being worked
on.
4. The fourth innovation is the development of technology that is no longer based
on (being secured with) Proof of Work, as within Bitcoin, but based on ‘Proof of
Stake’. The Proof of Work concept within Bitcoin becomes more and more dependent on a limited number of miner farms, especially in China. As a result, the
danger of centralizing power is lying in wait. The miners provide security and control on input references and double spending and are therefore the heart of the

174 See article by Vinay Gupta in the Harvard Business Review: 'A Brief History of Blockchain'

(28-2-2017).
175 English thinker, blogger and writer on the interface of (the history of) technology and philosophy. He is involved in, among other things, the Ethereum platform.
176 Bitcoin Core (BTC).
177 See also article in Fortune by Lucinda Shen: ‘Blockchain Will Be Used By 15% of Big Banks
By 2017’ (28-09-2016).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

194

Bitcoin protocol to prevent fraud. Proof of Stake is more based on the use of
complex financial tools that can realize the same or higher level of security.
5. According to Gupta, the fifth innovation on the horizon is: ‘blockchain scaling’.
Currently, each node must process transactions. This makes the total slow, nonefficient and therefore less scalable. According to him, a scalable blockchain must
improve the efficiency, without reducing the level of security. The protocol also
investigates how many nodes are required to validate each transaction and distributes the work across the nodes. The scalable blockchain is required, among other
things, to be able to settle transactions on the Internet of Things.
To pick up on these latest developments: we expect that blockchain organizations
(including Decentralized Autonomous Organizations) and miners will also work via
the ancient principles of specializing, coordinating and finalizing. This means that
there will be specialized nodes within a shared transaction network, which can check
and process certain types of transactions. Other nodes will then process other types
of transactions. For example, a transaction of hundred million euros can be settled
differently than a transaction of 1 eurocent. The settlement of the transaction can
also depend on who the sellers and buyers are and their reputation. Machine Learning
and Artificial Intelligence will also play an important role within blockchain protocols. The coordinating mechanism continues to focus on organizing sufficient (mathematically substantiated) trust at minimum costs.
We expect that Vinay Gupta will agree with use, that there is an important role for
the government in the development of a blockchain infrastructure. It is our view,
and that of for example Mariana Mazzucato178, that it is not companies that will provide long term large social breakthroughs, but governments, whether or not in private-public cooperatives. In her book Mazzucato teaches us the following: “The cutting edge of innovation, particularly in infrastructure, is often in the hands of the state, and that
seems destined to be true in the blockchain space.”
Especially when it concerns the underlying basis infrastructure, data utility facility,
we believe that the government179, and in Europe we think of the ‘European Commission’ in particular, will be of decisive importance. The thought behind this is that
a ‘road network’ is required to process transactions (transport from supply to demand). Hereby it is important that there is standardization, network neutrality and
that there are traffic rules and an infrastructure no longer owned by a limited number
of commercial companies with other intentions. Companies only have to build a garden path (own application) from the front door to the road. Local roads can be built
by councils and national roads by the national government. Therefore, we have to
use a layered infrastructure within Blockchain Organizing. We believe that in the US
there is less cooperation between private and public and the big five (Amazon, Alphabet, Apple, Facebook en Microsoft) will try to preserve their position in the
178 See book by Mariana Mazzucato: ‘The Entrepreneurial State’ (2011).
179 More general: a commonality with democratic basis.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

195

organization of the data infrastructure. In countries like China and Russia, we expect
that the state will have an important stake in the development of a blockchain basis
infrastructure.
From our experiences in digital transformation and developing communities, we can
imagine several stages in the digital transformation and more specific in Blockchain
Organizing when we look at the aspect of central versus decentral organizing:
1. Central governance /database, without interface applications. For example: central database of a bank, no sequence problem for transactions and the bank can
choose who gets a bank account and who not.
2. Central database with interface. For example: banks in the PSD2180 era. In this
stage other entities can develop applications for consumers for example to access
financial information, independent form the bank or other financial service companies. Think of the aforementioned Electronic Financial Record, as part of your
Personal Data Service.
3. Company platform that facilitates 1:1 or 1:n transactions without interface application. For example Airbnb, Ubers or Booking.com. Still one party or central authority is leading in the supply chain and can impose discounts or higher cost for
other actors without a democratic decision system or consensus mechanism.
4. Company platform with application ecosystem and (API) interfaces, for example
Google with Yet Another Mail Merge and other application that work on Gmail
or Google calendar or maps.
5. Decentralized platform, for example Ethereum181 is managed by the non-profit
Ethereum Foundation and enables developers to develop decentral applications
(so called DAPPS) on this platform. In this case it is possible to design decisionsystems that require a 90% consensus before releasing a new version of the software, or forking in the transaction future.
6. Decentralized information and transaction network with no central authority or
single service provider. Governance is Trias Politica and in Europe, most likely,
investments are done in a public/private cooperation
7. Agnostic ecosystem: a shared information and transaction center independent
from the existing industries for transport, storage and repackaging of data in order
to give answers to digital questions. Working with a certain consensus on the ontology, taxonomy, synonym list and semantic procedures.
At this moment most of the organizations are still in the first phase where they work
with local, not well coordinated systems for communication and processing transaction with tools such as Microsoft Office, local (paper) forms, phone talks and face
to face meetings. The use of cloud service is not yet mainstream, for example. That
has almost nothing to do with the technology, but with the way we are used to organize work. Most people share information by sending emails with attachment ant
180 Payment Service Directive 2(PSD2): directive for banks to give third parties access to their

data.
181 Or for example Synereo that wants to build a peer-to-peer social platform without central authority such as Facebook.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

196

typing data in own Excel sheets or Word documents. When it comes to organizing
information processes, most of the organizations are still in the hunter-gathering period, some of them are in the crafts periods and only a few are working with an
assembly line for data processes. And that’s only information sharing. It is probably
a good idea that when you are going to design and develop a shared transaction system, is to skip some phase and bring information sharing immediately in a shared
transaction center and see sending and receiving data as a transaction (which it is!).
6.3 Where blockchain should not lead to
From the white paper by Nakamoto182, it appears that Satoshi Nakamoto has carefully considered the gap between the status quo of laws and regulations and the potential of the Bitcoin protocol. An example of this is the gradual and controlled
growth of the Bitcoin network. It should not go too quickly as it could endanger the
robustness of the network. If the network would become too big too quickly, sleeping dogs could be woken up. As far as robustness is concerned, we like to point out
an interesting technical characteristic of blockchain: a ‘young’ blockchain is quite vulnerable to external danger (hacking). Regarding the sleeping dogs, a technology that
has not yet reached its full potential and is exposed to (often commercial) short term
strategy, is at risk of being dismissed as unimportant or dangerous. This was also
tried with Bitcoin, by connecting it to criminal activities and the Dark web.
Premature exposure can also lead to 'quick money earners' getting in on the act and
applying the technique within their current thinking and management model, with
the only intention to turn this into cash as soon as possible. In that case, Bitcoin
mainly becomes a change of medium, just like internet. At this moment (beginning
of 2018) it seems that more traders, investors and financial professionals are busy
buying and selling cryptocurrencies then designers and developers who build blockchains. Instead of creating jobs in trading or worse speculating on something that
has to be developed: work on the design and development itselves is our believe. It
seems that greed (again) is becoming more important than understanding The following figures illustrates this situation which also occurs with blockchain start-ups,
although most entrepreneurs and in particular investors will deny this:

182 As well as the many conversations that he had afterwards with early interested parties such as

Hal Finney and Martti Malmi.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

197

Figure 6.1: ‘Greed versus understanding’ and new business models183

In addition to start-ups and investors, we also see questionable developments taking
place in existing companies. An almost epic example of this is the proposal of Accenture184 to make blockchains mutable. It is a proposal to make it possible to enable
changing written transactions. Admittedly, under an extremely strict regime, but still
the ultimate potential of blockchain will definitely be lost if this occurs. After all, one
of the main characteristics of blockchain is its immutability. But mistakes are human,
and we do not live in a perfect world, do we? Absolutely. But we believe that you
should not undo a transaction once it has been executed, by changing that same
transaction afterwards. What you should do is creating a new transaction that ‘repairs’ the ‘incorrect’ or ‘wrong’ transaction. That you ‘cannot undo’ the past in a nonmutable blockchain, or actually create transparency, is (still) dead scary to many people. But transparency is an important driver for a better and more democratic world.
183 Image from: NRC.Next and DeMorgen.

184 See also the PDF of Accenture: ‘Editing the un-editable blockchain: Why distributed ledger

technology must adapt to an imperfect world’ (2016).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

198

Once you know that you cannot change anything afterwards, you will carefully consider the consequences of an action or behavior in advance.
You might think that all of this is not so significant, a blockchain that may or may
not be mutable. But precisely this immutability is, in our view, a hard condition for
calling something a blockchain. Otherwise it would just be a central organized database in a world, where the reality for the seller and buyer does not have to be the
same. The development of including a newly designed technology, beneficial to all
people, in an existing social economic order, is of course not new. It is the old 'fight'
for power. But evolution teaches us that eventually not the existing organizing regime
or system will win, but the system that can best adapt to the changing circumstances
and the system with the lowest fiction costs.
Another development that (sometimes justifiable) is criticized by the existing establishment such as financial regulators, are the ICOs (Initial Coin Offerings). These are
somewhat similar to the IPO (Initial Public Offering), whereby a company registers
on the stock market and collects money by issuing shares. But an own coin or token
can, in addition to the function of share, also have the function of calculation unit or
currency within a community. Exchange markets subsequently ensure that you can
also exchange a cryptocurrency into dollars, for example.185 The criticism on ICOs
by regulators is that it is impossible to check, for example, the identity of the person
who is offering the ICO. Plus, there is a lot of speculation with coins. Comparisons
are drawn with Ponzi schemes and there is often a weak relationship between the
financial and real value of a company or project. These developments are also familiar. We saw the same thing occurring at the creation of internet. Technology alone
will not change people’s behavior.
Apart from these developments, we currently also see the occurrence of a fight ‘for
power’ between public blockchains (new establishment) and private blockchains (old
establishment). We saw the same fight when internet was introduced. The existing
establishment, the existing way of organizing supply and demand clearly won at that
time. Whether this will also happen with blockchain, still has to be proven. Hopefully, we have learned, and we will not make the same mistakes. Or as the famous
Dutch soccer player Johan Cruijff would have said: “Before I make a mistake, I know
that I did not make this mistake.” In other words: do not make the same mistake twice
and learn from the past. The early internet gives us a realistic picture of a decentralized democratic world in which information exchange is possible without the intervention of third parties and at minimum transaction costs. But the internet has
drifted far from its original intention. We all know the problems of the internet: fake
work, loss of privacy, power rests with a limited number of companies, fast spreading
of fake news, and it makes us insufficiently more productive. Companies, non-transparent financial markets and governments have become more powerful than even
185 At this moment (beginning 2018) there are already more than 1300 coins (also called Altcoins

in relation to the first: Bitcoin), see also: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cryptocurrencies

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

199

before due to internet. Internet has damaged our democracy rather than strengthened
it. Let’s not make this ‘mistake’ again with blockchain.
What is blockchain not and what are the disadvantages?
Regularly, you see dialogues on online discussion groups debating the question: what
is a successful blockchain application? But this question is similar to the question:
name a successful student, for example, of the Harvard University. To judge whether
the student is successful, he or she must have completed the university with a certificate, but then he or she is no longer a student. Even when the student has a certificate and starts to work, when will he or she be successful? Only when the learned is
implemented step by step or also when he or she interprets changing circumstances
and responds to learned principles? So, what is successful, how can you measure it?
Will the Bitcoin blockchain become less successful because many greedy speculators
use it to generate money with money?
The question is also who can claim the name ‘blockchain’ and what does it mean?
The name probably first appeared in the Cipher Block Chaining concept (see also
paragraph 3.1). Nakamoto does not use the term blockchain in the white paper. The
text is about ‘ongoing chain’, ‘honest chain’, ‘longest chain’, ‘proof-of-work chain’,
‘chain of blocks’, etcetera, but not directly about blockchain. However, it is clear that
this is meant but it is not called as such. So, when may/can a person claim a name
and meaning and what is the relevance of it at this moment? For us, blockchain and
mainly Blockchain Organizing is not something that already exists. It is not a fully
developed concept or a way of thinking yet. It is especially something that could
encourage you to think differently and anew.
We know situations whereby the CIO concludes that blockchain technology is not a
smart choice to solve a certain problem, while the CEO still wants to go for it. The
reason could be: Fear Of Missing Out. At this moment blockchain, and more specific
cryptocurrency, is definitely a hype and a growing number of directors wants to participate and join the discussions, without knowing the advantages and disadvantages.
We cannot prevent this behavior by publishing this book. It will probably lead to the
implementation of a stretched concept of 'blockchain' in such a way that it still solves
the problem and the CEO is eventually right. There are many discussions about the
question whether, when and how an organization will start with a blockchain pilot.
There is little thought and discussion about the why question. This turns blockchain
into a hammer looking for nails. An important part of the blockchain development
will also be the development of a conceptual framework so in the long term we will
talk about the same things.
In this book, we have written a lot about what blockchain and Blockchain Organizing
is, and less about what it is not. What Blockchain Organizing, and definitely blockchain technology, is not at all, is a panacea for all organizational and management
problems. Blockchain technology also has some disadvantages, such as:
▪ You require more storage capacity because all transactions are stored on all nodes.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

200

▪ You must pay connection costs between all nodes.
▪ To verify a transaction and to include this in a block with the help of miners, costs,
relatively, a lot of time (10 minutes within Bitcoin) and energy (power).
▪ It is relatively expensive to verify each transaction in the same way. A smart verification learns from previous verifications and you could probably also verify
batch-wise or let the type of verification depend on the reliability of the sender
and receiver.
▪ The protocol must be maintained and finding consensus is in some cases probably
more expensive than a hierarchy or dictatorship.
▪ Finally, you will have to replace existing systems, that have an almost totally opposite architecture. This cannot be done with maintenance or by continuously
adding new functionality, but is only possible with a new building.
Some companies start with their own blockchain (permissioned) without miners.
Verification can be done locally, but the governance of the protocol is central. Apart
from the fact, that we benefit little from local blockchains, demand and supply is still
not organized in a fundamentally different way. As a consumer you still have a lockin with a bank and for example as citizen a lock-in with a council and it is not always
a secure solution. After all, you can hack a so-called decentral network, that is centrally controlled, or the system can fail. Therefore, third parties might get full control
over the ‘so-called’ decentral nodes. When you, as hacker, take over the control, you
can also commit fraud. With the failure of a permissioned blockchain, political decisions can also influenced. Some participants will be treated differently from others.
For example, after the crisis in 2008 system, banks (shareholders) were rescued at the
expense of others (the tax payer).
If there are no (democratic or decentral) incentives within permissioned blockchains
to check transactions, why would you opt for blockchain technology? In that case, a
blockchain will be no more than a cumbersome and expensive database technology.
This means that Blockchain Organizing is much more about power and organizing
than about technology.
6.4 Innovation Strategy: towards a decentralized cooperation perspective
In traditional management programs at universities, strategy is often taught as a company’s coordinated effort to realize a chosen finalization. A strategy is a path to a
chosen destination so to speak. Strategies are often aimed at developing core competencies in order to gain a competitive advantage over competitors or startups. The
basic idea that a company or a tool in general, is a means to an end is forgotten.
Traditional innovation strategies are often designed and processed from a protective
perspective instead of a cooperative perspective (see also difference between Darwin’s and Kropotkin’s perspective in paragraph 3.5). A basis function of a hierarchy
is to coordinate supply and demand, to connect humans and resources to activities
in a way that productivity, and with that our standard of living, increases. The

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

201

fundamental goal of hierarchies was not to make rich people richer or stimulate inequality.
A traditional company is considered to have succeeded when it has implemented a
strategy that its competitors found too hard or too costly to imitate. So basically the
successful company will have more food than others and therefore more chances to
survive. But we humans are not an individual species, but a social one. And social
animals have a higher chance to survive. So at the end of the day, cooperation has
probably a higher chance to survive than competition. Most of the strategies that are
taught at business schools, ignore the fact that companies are just a means to an end,
invented by humans to improve our standard of living. If some other coordinating
mechanism between supply and demand is more efficient than the old one, the traditional one becomes irrelevant. And with this irrelevancy all kinds of MBA programs
and business schools with old ‘firm- DNA’ will become irrelevant, unless they adapt.
If we take a closer look at the relevance of ‘competition’ nowadays we see that it
becomes less relevant. Rosenberg Equities186 made an overview of the frequency of
use of the words ‘competition’, ‘competitors’ and ‘pressure’ in annual reports. The
frequency of the word ‘competition’ for example decreased from 8 times per 10,000
words in 1995, to less than 2 times per 10,000 words in 2017. The Economist concluded that two-thirds of American industries were more concentrated and in the
hands of a few firms, probably as a result of executed strategies they learned at business school (survival of the fittest).
Even if firms are competitors in a market, the equity is, more and more, held by a
few parties. This is, from the perspective of decentralized organizing, democracy and
self-sovereignty, not a desired development. But still a lot of traditional firms design
and act from a centralized and competitive perspective, instead of a more decentralized cooperative perspective. The organization of work and economics should not
be a matter of competition or cooperation, central or decentral, it should be about
the balance between them. What matters at the end of the day is to achieve a better
standard of living for more people. It’s about the question: How to connect people and
resources to finalization in a way that has the highest aggregated productivity for society?
In traditional companies managers have to invest enormous resources to protect a
traditional way of thinking as if the competitive advantage is the only strategy for
happiness, a higher standard of living, or a better life. This old way of thinking comes
with a disadvantage when a new situation emerges and when other strategies (decentralized cooperation: learn, innovate, be open, crowdsourcing and work together)
becomes more relevant. This traditional strategy partly explains, for example, Kodak’s decision to put the digital camera ‘on the shelf’ in order to protect its core
competence and cash cow. That didn’t work out well. Using a strategy of protecting
186 See also article: 'What annual reports say, or do not, about competition', The Economist (16-

11-2017).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

202

an existing competitive advantage, works in environments that have long term stability. However, such environments no longer exist.
The rapid pace of globalization, digital transformation and sustainability has changed
the dynamics in most industries. In addition to these developments, more and more
industries become ‘the same’ as we look at the way transactions can be designed and
performed in shared information and transaction networks. It doesn’t matter what
you order at Amazon, the underlying processes and logistics are basically the same
and as a consumer you don’t see whether or not it is a complex or simple process.
Most companies not only need to develop the ability to respond to change quickly,
but also to give up some autonomy and ‘learn’ to cooperate with former competitors.
Learn to give up some autonomy in order to gain more advantages per actor is very
difficult in a world that in the last hundred years was educated with old organizing
paradigms of protecting own revenue models and maximization of parts instead of
the whole. Introducing a new paradigm is difficult because traditional management
practices, that were needed to protect competitive advantages, are the opposite way
of increasing cooperative advantages in a decentralized autonomous environment.
Unless you want to be trapped in continuous reorganizations where your employees
are not interested in the next step anymore, leaders in, especially large organizations,
need to change their approach to strategy. Strategy can no longer be defined as the
singular exploitation of core competencies to maximize gains and protect existing
business models. We need authentic digital leaders, and organization designers to
‘see’ this and act on it. Traditional management and innovation strategies are at the
end of the S-curve: it doesn’t get any better anymore and things will get worse.
Modern organizations will have the ability to compete in mature markets with new
technologies and organization models. Blockchain Organizing and its design principles can help with this. Blockchain Organizing doesn’t start with competition and
protection but with cooperation and renewal. Stakeholders look for hygiene factors:
processes in which they can better work together instead of competing with each
other. And processes like data logistics, purchasing, invoicing and paying are not
processes you can compete with. Customers just expect that you do these processes
correctly with minimal cost. You can’t compete on doing your booking keeping correctly, you can only fail.
6.5 Blockchain in the near future
Since the Renaissance our social-economic establishment is more and more formed
by institutions that are aimed at reducing insecurity and organizing trust. When Ann
wants to buy a book from Ben, there are several parties involved in this transaction,
such as: a bank, an insurance company, a bookseller and an accountant to process
the transactions in the accounting system. In principle a trusted third party, such as
a notary when you sell or buy a house performs three processes: checking whether
Ann is the owner of the house she wants to transfer to Ben, whether she only spends

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

203

this value once and the entire process must go securely. Basically, we can automate
these three processes and we no longer need the notary (and other parties such as
bank and accountant). See figure 6.2:

Figure 6.2: Programmable trust replaces Trusted Third Parties

From the moment we are born till the moment we die, we have innovated all kinds
of organizations, with the intention to make our life more pleasant. Our value system
is more and more formed and is more and more dependent on tools like: money,
debts, insurances, pensions, economic and monetary policies, continuous economic
growth, trade agreements and institutions such as the IMF, the EU and the G8. These
institutions and tools have increasingly distanced themselves from most people and
lack a human dimension.
Now there is a tool that can organize trust, with the human dimension as principle.
We can make connections again, share information and do business, from person to
person, with less intermediaries. We can reverse the chain, from power with the companies, to power with the people. Figure 6.3 shows an example of what is possible
with Blockchain Organizing. Companies are leading in the old model. The same data
must be entered at different locations and a bunch of keys are needed to log in differently everywhere. In the new model, a company ‘pings’ a question to a Personal
Data Service. With the answer, this company can start its process, such as shipping
a book by Amazon. Amazon asks a question to your PDS: what is your ‘name’, ‘street
name’, ‘postal code’ and ‘place of residence’? In a millisecond, the Amazon system
receives an answer and it can start a business process. After this, these four attributes
no longer have to be present in the database of Amazon. So, in the new model people
bring their own data when they for place an order with a company. The company
can simply process this data because it is connected to the same data utility. You can
compare this with the power from your solar panels that is used by a company for
the lighting of an office.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

204

Figure 6.3: Old Organizing: organization in the lead

Figure 6.4: New Organizing: humans in the lead.

In the new situation organizations ‘asks’ questions to Personal Data Services (PDS)
and attributes are scattered over the network so there is less motivation to ‘hack’ a
server because it contains attributes of millions of different users without any relation
between the attributes and users. When we look at cybersecurity most people think
about tooling to protect data. But when a new tool is invented by the protector, the

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

205

aggressor will find a new tool that will be stronger. This action-reaction ‘game’ will
never end. So we need a more fundamental approach.
When an inspector tries to solve a crime, he or she will always look at three aspects:
tool, circumstances (change to get away with it) and motivation. In cybersecurity not
much people look at the motivation to steel data. When a thief steels a truck load he
or she will have a customer for the load, for example thousands of laptops. But when
attributes are scattered over the network and a server has attributes of millions different users, hacking a server would be like steeling a truck with thousands of different products, a truck full of laptops, cloths, food, construction materials etcetera.
There will be no customer for the truckload, so there will be no motivation to steel
despite circumstances and tooling. The new privacy law GDPR should not be about
more tooling and more institutions, but about another mind-set and IT-architecture.
We require less and less existing institutions. We can organize trust again without
increasing complexity and therefore distrust. Whether the tools, that are currently
available to us, will be used (properly) in the coming decades, will not so much depend on the technology, but on pioneers, authentic digital leadership and the political
and human willingness to fundamentally innovate the existing establishment for their
own benefit. Now with blockchain there is an advantage compared to the rise of
internet: time is starting to run out to solve a number of potentially disruptive, social
problems.
Of course, we do not have a crystal ball. We cannot look into the future and predicting how Blockchain Organizing will further develop itself, remains difficult. But we
do know that we determine the world in which we live, with the choices that we
make. We know that you can better organize in the future, when the existing world
is less complex. The world becomes less complex when we base ourselves on organizational principles and not just try to solve practical problems in a way that will cause
other problems. But simplicity does not provide sufficient employment, complexity
does. It seems that we refuse to accept a simpler world, because otherwise we have
nothing more to do. The world becomes less complex when we give another meaning to growth, to work and therefore also to unemployment. We have the technology
to work less, but we still want to keep everyone employed, so we create fake work
which makes the world unnecessarily complex.
Every intermediary in the system might become more productive with IT investments, but we continue to add new intermediaries, new parts in the system, because
we strive for full employment. These new intermediaries are subsequently badly coordinated which makes the world unnecessarily more complex and can no longer be
understood by a growing number of people. The only way to understand a complex
world is to review our identity with economized work and to go back to the organizational principles. This might be the most important feature of Blockchain Organizing; going back to the origin. Also, back to the origin of organizing and managing.
Many people have forgotten why we work and why we organize work. We only think

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

206

in permanent jobs or more or less flex jobs or how we regulate them. We are so busy
with economics that we forget to ask ourselves some more philosophical questions.
Or as the ACM Turing Award winner Alan Kay puts it: “Our (real) job is to remind us
that there are more contexts than the one that we’re in, the one that we think is reality.”
Reinvention of management
Till now we had to use a management system that was invented at the beginning of
the twentieth century. We think that management innovates, but these are often small
adjustments within the existing invention. The system itself, with the firm as superior
organizational form, has not changed. Dominant management theories also do not
contribute to the change thereof. Management science is still mainly seen as a form
of applied social science, while a large part of the tasks that we perform as a person,
can be put in a technical protocol, which, with mathematical proof, can perform tasks
must more accurate, more secure and quicker. With organizational technology, the
field of expertise of management science and organization theory will also change
and can become more of a hard science.
Management and therefore all kinds of management resources, such as books,
courses, consultancy and assessments, will also fundamentally change. We, as a person, have become more productive because in the last hundred years we have started
to use technology, including in particular management technology. We’ll become less
productive when we use the wrong tools. The latter is clearly visible in the productivity figures since the 1970s, when a lot of people started to work in offices. At this
moment, most management tools make our world more complex which costs us
more time and attention for something that can also be automated.
We can more easily imagine what the negative consequences of robotics are than the
positive effects for our busy lives. We can get rid of institutions that we use to organize trust and create fake work or non-meaningful work. Trust is the lubricant for
the organization of work and economy. But when this lubrication turns into sand,
things will go wrong with the ‘machine’ that we use to produce welfare. With this
book, we have tried to offer a basis for a new social-economic establishment with
organizational technology, and more specific with Blockchain Organizing as a tool
that can contribute to this. That is not yet easy for everyone to imagine. This is not
because Blockchain Organizing is abstract, but because it differs from what we are
used to.
What does success depend on?
The success of blockchain technology will depend a lot on the question whether we
can achieve standardization in the short term. The TCP/IP protocol ensures that
internet could get off the ground quickly. Due to standardization, more and more
people will invest in this and therefore more and more people will start to use it and
the number of applications will increase. There is already some sort of standard for
the organizational model of Blockchain Organizing that reasons from the smallest

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

207

building blocks and organizational principles that have been around for a long time.
With regards to the use of the developed ontology and taxonomy, additions will definitely follow when more communities will start to use this as a basis in their own
context. In addition, the development will also depend on the acceptance by people.
People like to be in control and with Blockchain Organizing you might quickly get
the impression that you, as a person, are no longer in control and that the protocol
decides. It will scare many people that they cannot implement mutations in transactions that have taken place. Algorithm aversion could also cause a delay. We, as persons, sooner rely on people than on algorithms, even when it has been proven that
the latter are better.
A main conclusion of this book is that Blockchain Organizing, like internet, will not
fundamentally change a lot if we do not simultaneously change the model that we
use to organize supply and demand. Blockchain Organizing is based on a technology
whereby the ability is strongly reduced the more it is used within the walls of companies. Internet is a great tool to bring supply and demand together, without almost
any friction. But this technology, without the additional help of a new organizational
model, is insufficiently capable of solving problems in terms of fake work, privacy,
propaganda and low production growth for example. Science, democracy and law
are more and more undermined, and we ‘make’ large companies and central government more and more powerful. This is not due to the technology, but due to our
awareness, lack of trying to understand, behavior and due to the form in which the
technology is used. Technology is just a lever. You also need a foundation or common ground to stand on.
Blockchain Organizing offers foundations for apparently different organizational
and management problems, which often have the same origin. Problems in
healthcare, home care or education are exposed in different ways, while they often
still have the same origin. Usually the undercurrent of the problems that we experience, is not mentioned in the media. That is too abstract. Apart from these organizational problems in the public sphere, there are also problems in the private sphere
that are solved with old thinking and old organizing. These problems often have the
same cause and undercurrent: insufficient coordination of supply and demand and
solving problems with the same level of thinking that created them in de first place.
Often driven by political motives, opportunistic behavior and wanting to maintain
the existing power base. When you want to solve an organizational problem and can
let go of the following assumptions, you will make some progress:
▪ the firm and government as ideal form for organizing work an commonalities;
▪ working hard and continuous economic growth are good;
▪ competing has the exclusive rights to innovation and progress;
▪ innovating in combination with full employment is a good idea.
Blockchain Organizing offers principles to solve these problems in a different and
structural way. The advantage of principles is that these do not change very often.
The disadvantage is that many people find them abstract. Stephen Covey once said:

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

208

“There are three constants in life: change, choices and principles.” The world changes, therefore
we must choose and when you choose you can best do this from a number of principles and foundations. Because you do not need so many foundations to solve organizational problems, an organizational expert can be of great significance for the
problems that will keep us more and more busy. When you understand these foundations and apply them, you can handle almost every management problem.
Technically, there are still (especially with public blockchains) some problems to be
solved such as: centralization of mining capacity, speed of transaction processing,
synchronization and scalability. Security also remains an action-reaction game because whatever security is developed, eventually this can probably be cracked again.
The question is at what costs? But currently, not many fundamental technical problems are expected for Blockchain Organizing. The speed of development will be determined more by the adaptability of people: (still not enough people take the time
for a fundamental deepening of their understanding187), and the adaptability of existing:
▪ organizations that must disinvest and switch to another standard;
▪ powers that benefit from changing as little as possible;
▪ intermediaries that live from a complex world;
▪ standards and legislation.
In the chain between first seller and last buyer, Blockchain Organizing cannot perform all tasks autonomous. We need people, not just for inventing and designing
processes, but also for the input of values in a blockchain (so called ‘Oracles’), solving
errors or for example mediation in case of disputes. According to us, the following
is important for Blockchain Organizing in the short term: being aware of the problems, trust that we can do it, change of behavior, applying new organizational forms,
standardization (in identity management and blockchain protocol), algorithm aversion, regulation and legislation.
6.6 The future of Blockchain Organizing
When we look a little further into the future of Blockchain Organizing, psychology
(identity), anthropology (our connection with other people) and philosophy (asking
the right questions) will become increasingly more important. An important question
is for example: what is progress?
What is progress?
An important driver for people to make an effort is the expectation that we make
progress. But maybe for the first time in our existence we ask ourselves the question:
what is progress? When do we grow? When will our children be better off? Is it more
or better? Is the sustainability of what we have also growth? We believe it is. And as
we have shown in many places in this book, Blockchain Organizing contributes to
growth. If we become more productive and continue to produce the same, we have
187 Except, of course, the readers of this book.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

209

more surplus time and attention for the things that really matters to us and for sustainability and well-being. We think, that we will move more and more from the
offices with fake work to our homes, neighborhoods and community’s for real
‘work’. Do not take neighborhood literally. It can also be an online community.
For us as authors, an important condition to putting energy into something is an
answer to the following question: ‘Does the innovation contribute to a better future
for our children?’ However, better is a subjective concept. But we are convinced that
more and more people in the West will find that better mainly means sustainability
of what we have. The welfare crisis, we believe we are still experiencing, will actually
only stop if we can image a new reality and have a plan to achieve this. The crisis is
only over when we have accepted a new establishment that no longer strives for
more welfare, but a sustainable welfare or well-being. When this would be possible
within a few decades, it would be a success for the current generation of policy makers, CEO’s, politicians and mainly pioneers. When this is not possible, we are probably part of the first generation in modern times that ensures that the next generation
is not better off.
Beyond the hype
At the moment (beginning of 2018) you can definitely call the blockchain development, and more particularly: cryptocurrencies a hype. But a hype with much potential. A hype is usually characterized by ‘blown up’ expectations and no real results.
Not in the least by investors, crypto traders, dealers and consultants who believe they
can quickly make some money. But there are also 'world improvers', who want it
differently this time. People that experienced the internet development, also at the
core a decentralized and democratic system, and after decades concluded that we
drifted away from the basic idea. Or as Cypherpunk Jameson Lopp puts it: “The older
you are, the less excuse you have to be skeptical of the positive benefits of technological innovation
because you've witnessed the revolution firsthand over the previous decades.”
The question is whether anarchist of today becomes the traditional CEO’s of tomorrow? Do they ‘succumb’ to big money? Blockchain will probably also follow the
‘Hype Cycle for Emerging Technologies’ by Gartner188. The process of this cycle
usually looks as follows: at first hardly anyone knows the technology, except of
course the ‘inventor’ and a surrounding small group of ‘tech nerds’. Then someone
manages to persuade the media to write about it, the expectations increase, investors
and traders do not want to miss out and consultants will give lectures and provide
advice. But the technology remains vague for many people and the useable applications fail to turn up. So, the expectations drop. Only a few years after the peak of the
hype cycle, the technology gets sufficient traction because a standard is created, the
strongest ideas survive and the number of application increases.

188 See for example: ‘Gartner's 2016 Hype Cycle for Emerging Technologies Identifies Three Key

Trends That Organizations Must Track to Gain Competitive Advantage’ (16-08-2016).

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

210

The development of Blockchain Organizing is a different story and not the same as
the development of blockchain technology. One of the pillars of Blockchain Organizing (the organizational model: Weconomics) has already had its dip and since 2010
it has been on the rise with real projects and an increasing number of participants.
However, Blockchain Organizing, like ICT and internet, is also still in its infancy. We
are still hunting for information and we still do not have an internet assembly line.
There will certainly still be projects that fail. We learn from this. The development
of the assembly line for example, did also not immediately go well. Henry Ford went
bankrupt several times and especially the Second World War and the need for mass
production ensured a real breakthrough.
So also the circumstances can make or break an innovation. Internet did also not
immediately go well. For example think of: World Online, Myspace, Pets.com, and
Netscape Navigator. Blockchain Organizing will develop itself in the same way: by
trial and error. The technology will find its way in this, it has always done so. The
question is what we, as a person, will do with this technology. As a person, we have
always struggled socially to put new technologies into place. That will not be different
now. Not the technology, but man is the most important part in Blockchain Organizing. The examples of using Blockchain Organizing, which you have been able to
read in this book, can best be seen as examples to make you think and change your
perspective on management and organizations. Most examples in this book are not
tested in terms of answers to the question whether an idea or plan is actually implemented, still exists or is sustainable. For us, Blockchain Organizing is not something
that already exists, or a way of thinking, but much more a concept that makes you
think. This also applies to this book.
Organizing the future?
Based on recognizing patterns from the past and extending these patterns to our
future, we can make a reasonable assumption of the type of work that we will do,
how we will organize this and which management concepts we will require for this.
As a result of this we can make a reasonable assumption of the type of welfare that
we moving to, how we will organize this and what the contribution of Blockchain
Organizing can be. We can translate this into a long term vision. We plead that political parties189, in addition to a short-term plan (one to four years ahead) also make
a ‘next generation plan’ (three decades ahead). Every four years the short-term plans
should be tested to the original long-term plan. This double track decision must ensure that we do not cause long-term problems by solving short-term problems.
Now we can hear you say: how can we look forward one generation if we cannot
even look forward one year? This is thinking from an old paradigm that the world is
complex and dynamic and that there would not be any fundamental and recognizable
patterns. But these are there and have been described in detail in chapter 8 of the
189 Apart from political parties, NGOs and companies, for example, should also do this.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

211

Weconomics analysis book: ‘Patterns that predict the future.’ It is not always clear
how quickly patterns will develop themselves, but the direction usually is. So, our
development more and more focuses on wanting to control our environment, in
order to increase our survival rate. After controlling fire, controlling food production
and muscle strength, it is now the turn of information, and later on our welfare, to
be controlled. We do not longer want to hunt for information, but be able to acquire
this in a controlled manner. Because we cannot imagine that our (working) life ends
as shown in the figure below.

Figure 6.5: Evolution of working man

The next stage of the ‘working’ person
We will not be in front of the computer for decades. The question is what comes
after the information worker? We believe that we will initially mainly focus on the
smarter organization of the work of the information worker (sphere 3 in figure 6.6).
More and more data will soon be processed into information by a ‘blockchain assembly line’.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

212

Figure 6.6: Development to a fourth ‘working’ sphere (The productivity growth numbers, 5% 2.3% and <1%, come from the Netherlands but are comparable in other OECD countries)

The farmer (sphere 1; 3% now works here), was able to become more productive,
not just because the horse and carriage were replaced by the tractor, but also because
‘the surplus’ was able to work in factories: you mainly become more productive when there is
a replacement for your surplus time when technology makes you more productive. The same happened in the factory (sphere 2, 26% now works here). The assembly line made us
more productive and the surplus went to offices (sphere 3, 71% now works here).
More than 70% of the work force in The Netherlands now works in offices, but we
will not become more productive if the surplus cannot go somewhere (sphere 4). We
see that the productivity growth in sphere 3 (less than 1%) is far too little to keep up
with rising welfare costs (often more than 2%).
Generally, you could say that sustainability (sphere 4) mainly requires time and attention and Blockchain Organizing creates this. Therefore, man will also become, in
addition to (professional) specialist, a (private) generalist, so a hybrid day or week
format. But that is great, we like change and want to continue to develop ourselves.
Information workers will become more productive due to Blockchain Organizing,
which creates surplus time for sustainability. ICT becomes more and more mature
and the processing of data becomes more and more sustainable, this needs less attention. The privacy can be assured better, we become less dependent on a limited
number of data companies from Silicon Valley and preventing fake news becomes
easier. As a person, we are chrono-centric. We live in this age and think that Twitter,
Facebook and Google will be there forever. But before e-mail and social media existed, there were the: petroglyph, smoke signals, printing, letter, telegraph, telex and
fax. And now we have e-mail and social media to communicate. So, there is something before Facebook and Twitter... and there will be something afterwards.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

213

The next generation of (technical) innovations will continue to build on the existing
internet technology, but hopefully with more social relevance and a higher democratic level. Inventions that do not solve a social problem, will not survive long. So,
there will be new communication tools whereby we probably do not have to submit
our personal data. Tools that are less driven by adverts and consumption. The advert
machines, that now make ‘free’ communication and sharing possible (but also fake
news), will lose meaning and be replaced. Anyway, a shared network is about and
turns the model around: in exchange for a few bucks per month:
▪ you remain the owner of your personal data that is worth a lot more;
▪ you receive discounts on orders because adverts are no longer required;
▪ you can select news, information, products and services more objectively.
But… first there is a lot to learn
As long as there is so much free money available in the market, investments in nonuseful applications will remain possible. A current IPO that is symbolic for this is
that of Snap Inc. This company is listed on the stock exchange at a value of 28 billion
dollars. It is not clear yet to us, what the social relevance is of the main application
of this company: Snapchat. At this moment, it is mainly used to take selfies and to
do sexting.
Investors are wild about a company that suffers losses190, whereby only the founders
have voting right and whereby it is also not clear how they will make a profit. Apparently, investors have learned little from similar IPOs like Twitter. Apparently, there
is so much cheap money in the economy191, that money flows to this type of companies. This is in stark contrast to the somewhat less sexy companies in, for example,
healthcare, that do solve real social problems, but that find it more difficult to find
funds because they are probably less speculative and ‘sexy’. We can no longer imagine
that, partly due to pension funds, money is still being invested in companies that are
so far away from the real problems that we face as humans and society. Or would
our problems be so great that it would be better to go down while ‘snapchatting’?
With Blockchain Organizing we prefer to work on solving real organization problems. With the use of Blockchain Organizing we will organize the work of information workers less and less within the walls of companies and more and more
within shared information and transaction networks. Not companies, but humans
and protocols are important for the organization of our work. Money and contracts
will be less and less required and therefore also all kinds of institutions, likes banks.
Man is increasingly becoming a prosumer and can no longer be put in a ‘box’ as
citizen, consumer or employee. The artificial distinction between the personal, private and public spheres will disappear more and more in the organization of work,
welfare and wellbeing.

190 In 2016 more than half a billion dollars.
191 Partly thanks to the QE programs and opening the money tap by the FED and ECB.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

214

Siloization will only lead to more complexity and distrust. Man is above all human
and will organize trust and work, more and more via shared transaction networks.
Because institutionalization has gone too far, and the welfare state has become too
expensive, more and more people will (have to) perform more tasks themselves, instead of outsourcing these to a private or public sector. This is not just for economic
reasons it is also embedded in our being. We are social animals and time and attention
for our environment is still worth the most. We can only conclude that the current
social-economic order imprisons us into something that we are not. Man is therefore
the only animal species that refuses to be what it is as the French philosopher Albert
Camus formulated. Man is the only animal species that requires intelligence to be in
balance with his environment. Linear rationalization has brought us to a point of
irrationality and dis-rationality that borders on a new form of ridiculousness. Our
innovations have brought us to a crossroad, where choosing the right way is more
important than ever.
What is success? The identity of the working person
During the House of Representatives elections in 2017 in the Netherlands, 'the
Dutch identity' was an important theme192. We understand this, but will we find out,
in the near future, that this type of identity issues contrasts sharply with a completely
different identity issue. For a long time, it was clear what progress entails. That threatens to change for the first time in our modern history. Chances are that our children
will no longer be better off in the sense of more welfare. But the question is then:
what is better? This is also a form of identity. What is more successful and will this
make us happy? When Albert Einstein was traveling to Japan in 1922 to give a series
of lectures, he heard that he was awarded for the Nobel Prize. He was overwhelmed
with all the attention of people who wanted to meet him. Back in the safe environment of his hotel room he wanted to give a messenger a tip. The messenger either
refused to accept the tip, in line with local practice, or Einstein had no small change
available to thank the messenger. Instead of giving money he wrote a note to the
messenger: “A calm and modest life brings more happiness than the pursuit of success combined
with constant restlessness”.
For how long can we still identify ourselves with more success, more welfare, income,
paid work and consume more and more? Will it make us happy or can we also develop another identity? Anyway, our western way of life, our human identity is at
stake. This goes much further than our own sovereign state identity. Sovereignty is a
relative concept when you think from the starting point: human self-sovereignty. As
we saw earlier in this book, Ubuntu means I am because we are. The question what
‘we is’, what we want and what see as progress are very difficult questions to answer,
especially because these questions where never asked. We have always been busy
with gathering food, hunting, production of food and products and processing data
into information.
192 Same ‘identity’ issues we saw at the French and German election and the aftermath of Brexit

referendum in 2017.

Boek8_Kopie_Intern_Gebruik_Weconomics

215

More and more people do not know who they are, what they want and where they
are going. We will form a new identity because economically and materially you cannot grow infinitely on a finite planet. Claiming or implementing the opposite should
be made a criminal offence, as far as we are concerned. We start looking for answers
to questions like: Who am I and what do I want? With which welfare do I want to
live? What is my relationship to myself, to others, to my job, to technology and especially to the future of our children? People become increasingly aware of the consequences of unnecessary use of scarce raw materials and tools.
We hope that tools will become more functional again and that there is less money
